while the composite
\rho\tau^{b}
is the vertical cofiber of the outer rectangle in

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{0,-a-b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{0,-a-b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{0,-a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{0,-b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{0,0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{S}^{0,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{a+b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

so it reduces to producing an isomorphism between the two squares. In general, it would be difficult as one has to compare the two filling homotopies. However, in this case both squares lie in the image of the monoidal functor
\gamma\colon\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}\to\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
, and their preimages can both be identified with the unique square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-a-b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-a}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

since the source is a
1
-category in which every commutative square has a unique filler.
∎

Remark 2.16
.

This result is not as innocent as it seems. For instance, taking
a=b
, it implies
\cdot\tau^{a}\colon\Sigma^{0,-a}X/\tau^{a}\to X/\tau^{a}
is nullhomotopic, which is a bit surprising compared to the fact in
\mathsf{Sp}
that
\cdot 2\colon\mathbb{S}^{0}/2\to\mathbb{S}^{0}/2
is not nullhomotopic. The above argument does not work in the Moore spectrum situation because there is no
monoidal
functor
\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}\to\mathsf{Sp}
sending
-1\to 0
to
\cdot 2\colon\mathbb{S}^{0}\to\mathbb{S}^{0}
, or equivalently (cf. Theorem
2.67
)
\mathbb{S}^{0}/2
does not admit the structure of an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-algebra in
\mathsf{Sp}
.

Fact 2.17
.

It is clear from construction that
\tau^{a}\tau^{b}=\tau^{a+b}
,
\rho_{a}^{a+b}\rho_{a+b}^{a+b+k}=\rho_{a}^{a+b+k}
and
\rho_{a}^{a+b}\delta_{k}^{a+b}=\delta_{k}^{a}
.

Lemma 2.18
.

For any
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
,
1\leqslant a\leqslant\infty,0\leqslant b<\infty,0\leqslant k\leqslant\infty
, we have a cartesian square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,-b}X/\tau^{a+k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{a+k+b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,-b}X/\tau^{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{a+b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{b}}

Proof.

Such a square is constructed by taking horizontal fibers of the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{a+k+b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{a+b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

and it is cartesian since the vertical right arrow of the latter square is an isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 2.19
.

Under the same setup, if
a,k<\infty
, there is also a commutative square in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,-b}X/\tau^{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{a+b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{1,-a-b}X/\tau^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{1,-a-b}X/\tau^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{a+b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

deduced from the original square by taking vertical cofibers.

Lemma 2.20
.

For
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
and
0\leqslant a\leqslant\infty,1\leqslant b<\infty,0\leqslant k<\infty
, we have a cartesian square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{b+k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{1,-b-k}X/\tau^{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{1,-b}X/\tau^{a+k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{b+k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{b}}

Proof.

Such a square is constructed by taking horizontal cofibers of the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{a+k+b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{b+k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{a+k+b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}

and it is cartesian since the vertical left arrow of the latter square is an isomorphism.
∎

2.2   SS and the Bockstein dictionary

Definition 2.21
.

A
spectral sequence (SS)
of abelian groups
E=\{E^{s,t}_{r}\}
consists of the following data:

•

For each
r\geqslant 2
,
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
, an abelian group
E_{r}^{s,t}
.

•

For each
r\geqslant 2
,
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
, a map of abelian groups
d_{r}\colon E_{r}^{s,t}\to E_{r}^{s+r,t+r-1}
, so that
d_{r}d_{r}=0
.

•

For each
r\geqslant 2
,
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
, an isomorphism
\frac{\ker(d_{r}\colon E_{r}^{s,t}\to E_{r}^{s+r,t+r-1})}{\IIm(d_{r}\colon E_{r}^{s-r,t-r+1}\to E_{r}^{s,t})}\cong E_{r+1}^{s,t}
.

A map between SS
E\to E^{\prime}
is a family of maps of abelian groups
E_{r}^{s,t}\to(E^{\prime})_{r}^{s,t}
that commutes with the
d_{r}
maps and the structural isomorphisms. These assemble into a
1
-category
\mathsf{SS}
.

Remark 2.22
.

When drawing a SS in the plane, we take
n=t-s
(resp.
s
) as the horizontal (resp. vertical) coordinate. Thus a
d_{r}
-differential in the picture moves one step to the left and
r
steps up.

Remark 2.23
.

Given a SS
E=\{E_{r}^{s,t}\}
, we refer to the maps
d_{r}
as the
differentials
on the
E_{r}
-page. Furthermore, by lifting subquotients, we can produce a canonical family of subgroup inclusions for each
E_{2}^{s,t}
:

0=B_{1}^{s,t}\subset B_{2}^{s,t}\subset B_{3}^{s,t}\subset\cdots\subset B_{r}^{s,t}\subset\cdots\subset Z_{r}^{s,t}\subset\cdots\subset Z_{3}^{s,t}\subset Z_{2}^{s,t}\subset Z_{1}^{s,t}=E_{2}^{s,t}

so that
E_{r+1}^{s,t}\cong Z_{r}^{s,t}/B_{r}^{s,t}
for each
r\geqslant 1
. We refer to
Z_{r}^{s,t}
(resp.
B_{r}^{s,t}
) as the group of
d_{r}
-cycles
(resp.
d_{r}
-boundaries
) at bidegree
(s,t)
. By construction, any map of SS
f\colon E\to E^{\prime}
induces a family of maps between
d_{r}
-cycles (resp.
d_{r}
-boundaries).

To each filtered spectrum
X
we can associate a “standard SS”
E_{*}^{*,*}(X)
. We start with a lemma:

Lemma 2.24
.

Suppose
\mathcal{C}
is a stable
\infty
-category and there is a cartesian square in
\mathcal{C}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}

then for each homological functor
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{A}
, we have canonical identifications in
\mathcal{A}
:

\displaystyle\IIm(F(A)\to F(D))

\displaystyle\cong\frac{F(A)}{\ker(F(A)\to F(B))\oplus\ker(F(A)\to F(C))}

\displaystyle\cong\frac{\IIm(F(A)\to F(B))}{\IIm(\ker(F(A)\to F(C))\to F(A)\to F(B))}

\displaystyle\cong\frac{\IIm(F(A)\to F(C))}{\IIm(\ker(F(A)\to F(B))\to F(A)\to F(C))}.

Proof.

Write
X=\fib(A\to B)=\fib(C\to D)
and
Y=\fib(A\to C)=\fib(B\to D)
. We will prove that
X\to A
,
Y\to A
and
A\to D
fit into a cofiber sequence
X\oplus Y\to A\to D
. Consider the diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}

Taking vertical fibers for these, we obtain fiber sequences
X\to\fib(A\to D)\to Y
and
Y\to\fib(A\to D)\to X
so that the composites
X\to\fib(A\to D)\to X
and
Y\to\fib(A\to D)\to Y
are
\mathrm{id}
. From these we obtain an isomorphism
\fib(A\to D)\cong X\oplus Y
compatible with their maps into
A
. Taking the
F(-)
long exact sequence for
X\oplus Y\to A\to D
yields the first isomorphism we want, while the other two follow by a straightforward image computation in
\mathcal{A}
.
∎

Remark 2.25
.

Under the above setup, the same argument also proves a dual statement:

\displaystyle\IIm(F(A)\to F(D))

\displaystyle\cong\ker(F(D)\to F(D)/F(B)\oplus F(D)/F(C))

\displaystyle\cong\ker(\IIm(F(B)\to F(D))\to\IIm(F(B)\to F(D)\to F(D)/F(C)))

\displaystyle\cong\ker(\IIm(F(C)\to F(D))\to\IIm(F(C)\to F(D)\to F(D)/F(B))).

Theorem 2.26
.

For each
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
there is a
standard SS

E_{*}^{*,*}(X)=\{E_{r}^{s,t}(X)\}_{r\geqslant 2}
as follows:

1.

E_{2}^{s,t}(X)=\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
.

2.

For
r\geqslant 2
, each
E_{r}^{s,t}(X)
is a quotient of
\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})
. Actually,
E_{r}^{s,t}=Z_{r-1}^{s,t}/B_{r-1}^{s,t}
, where

•

Z_{r-1}^{s,t}\subset E_{2}^{s,t}=\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
is the image of
\rho\colon\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
.

•

B_{r-1}^{s,t}\subset E_{2}^{s,t}=\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
is the image of
\delta_{r-2}\colon\pi_{t-s+1,t-r+2}(X/\tau^{r-2})\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
.

Here we adopt the convention
Z_{1}^{s,t}=E_{2}^{s,t}
and
B_{1}^{s,t}=0
.

3.

|d_{r}|=(r,r-1)
for each
r\geqslant 2
. For
x\in Z_{r-1}^{s,t}
, if
[x]\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})
is a lift of
x
, then
\delta_{r-1}[x]\in\pi_{t-s-1,t+r-1}(X/\tau)=E_{2}^{s+r,t+r-1}(X)
is a representative of
d_{r}(x)
.

Furthermore, these assemble into a functor
E_{*}^{*,*}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{SS},X\mapsto E_{*}^{*,*}(X)
.

Proof.

Consider the following square in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{r-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,r-2}X/\tau^{2r-3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,r-2}X/\tau^{r-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{r-2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{r-1}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{2r-3}_{r-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{r-2}}

which is cartesian by Lemma
2.18
. Here the composite
\tau^{r-2}\rho^{r-1}_{1}\colon X/\tau^{r-1}\to\Sigma^{0,r-2}X/\tau^{r-1}
is homotopic to
\cdot\tau^{r-2}
by Proposition
2.14
. Applying Lemma
2.24
to this square, we conclude that the image of
\cdot\tau^{r-2}\colon\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})\to\pi_{t-s,t-r+2}(X/\tau^{r-1})
is
\IIm(\rho^{r-1}_{1})/\IIm(\rho^{r-1}_{r-2}\delta_{r-2}^{r-1})=Z_{r-1}^{s,t}/B_{r-1}^{s,t}=E_{r}^{s,t}
. Furthermore, we have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{r-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,r-2}X/\tau^{r-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{1,1-r}X/\tau^{r-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{1,-1}X/\tau^{r-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\tau^{r-2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\tau^{r-2}}

since
-\otimes\tau^{r-2}
defines a natural transformation. Taking
\pi_{t-s,t}
, we then obtain the expected
d_{r}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{r}^{s,t}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s,t-r+2}(X/\tau^{r-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s-1,t+r-1}(X/\tau^{r-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{r}^{s+r,t+r-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s-1,t+1}(X/\tau^{r-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}}

from functoriality of the epi-mono factorization applied to the horizontal arrows. It remains to show
d_{r}d_{r}=0
and
\ker(d_{r})/\IIm(d_{r})\cong E_{r+1}^{s,t}
. Actually,
d_{r}d_{r}=0
because
\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}\simeq\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}\rho^{2r-2}_{r-1}\delta_{r-1}^{2r-2}\simeq 0
due to Fact
2.17
. Furthermore, the composite map

\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})\xrightarrow{\rho_{r-1}^{r}}\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})\to E_{r}^{s,t}

factors through an epimorphism onto
\ker(d_{r})\subset E_{r}^{s,t}
, since
\rho^{r-1}_{1}\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}\rho^{r}_{r-1}\simeq\delta_{r-1}^{1}\rho^{r}_{r-1}\simeq 0
and the two maps
\rho_{r-1}^{r}\colon\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})
,
\tau\colon\pi_{t-s,t+1}(X/\tau^{r-2})\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})
collectively surject onto the kernel of
\tau^{r-2}\rho_{1}^{r-1}\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}\simeq\tau^{r-2}\delta_{r-1}^{1}\simeq\delta_{1}^{r-1}\rho_{1}^{r-1}\colon\pi_{t-s-1,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})\to\pi_{t-s-1,t+1}(X/\tau^{r-1})
due to an application of Lemma
2.24
on the cartesian square witnessing
\tau^{r-2}\delta_{r-1}^{1}\simeq\delta_{1}^{r-1}\rho_{1}^{r-1}
from Lemma
2.20
. A dual argument (replacing Lemma
2.24
by Remark
2.25
) shows the composite

E_{r}^{s,t}\to\pi_{t-s,t-r+2}(X/\tau^{r-1})\xrightarrow{\tau}\pi_{t-s,t-r+1}(X/\tau^{r})

factors through a monomorphism out of
E^{s,t}_{r}/\IIm(d_{r})
. Splicing these together, we obtain

\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})\to\ker(d_{r})\to\ker(d_{r})/\IIm(d_{r})\to E^{s,t}_{r}/\IIm(d_{r})\to\pi_{t-s,t-r+1}(X/\tau^{r})

which serves as an epi-mono factorization of the composite
\tau\tau^{r-2}\rho^{r-1}_{1}\rho^{r}_{r-1}\simeq\cdot\tau^{r-1}\colon\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})\to\pi_{t-s,t-r+1}(X/\tau^{r})
. Thus,
\ker(d_{r})/\IIm(d_{r})\cong E_{r+1}^{s,t}
by uniqueness of the epi-mono factorization.

Finally, the functoriality of
E_{*}^{*,*}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{SS}
is straightforward from the construction.
∎

In fact, the definition of SS makes sense for every abelian
1
-category. On the other hand, if
\mathcal{E}
is a presentable stable
\infty
-category, then the Lurie tensor product
\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})=\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
admits a
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
-action, so the notions of
\Sigma^{n,w}X=X\otimes\mathbb{S}^{n,w}
,
X/\tau^{r}
, and the fundamental distinguished triangle also make sense in
\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})
; see Definition
2.55
and Construction
2.58
for details. The argument in Theorem
2.26
thus yields the following general result.

Corollary 2.27
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable stable
\infty
-category,
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
be an abelian
1
-category and
\pi_{0}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
be a homological functor.
3
3

3

In practice, usually
\mathcal{E}
is a
t
-
\infty
-category, and
\pi_{0}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
takes the
0
-th homotopy object in its heart.
We denote by
\pi_{n}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
(
n\in\mathbb{Z}
) the composite
\pi_{0}\circ\Sigma^{-n}
. Then for each
X\in\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})
, there is an
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}\!
-valued standard SS

E_{*}^{*,*}(X)=\{E_{r}^{s,t}(X)\}_{r\geqslant 2}
as follows:

1.

E_{2}^{s,t}(X)=\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
. Here by
\pi_{n,w}Y
we mean
\pi_{n}(Y(w))\in\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
.

2.

For
r\geqslant 2
, each
E_{r}^{s,t}(X)
is a quotient of
\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})
. Actually,
E_{r}^{s,t}=Z_{r-1}^{s,t}/B_{r-1}^{s,t}
, where

•

Z_{r-1}^{s,t}\subset E_{2}^{s,t}=\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
is the image of
\rho\colon\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
.

•

B_{r-1}^{s,t}\subset E_{2}^{s,t}=\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
is the image of
\delta_{r-2}\colon\pi_{t-s+1,t-r+2}(X/\tau^{r-2})\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
.

Alternatively,
E_{r}^{s,t}
is the middle term in the epi-mono factorization of
\cdot\tau^{r-2}\colon\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})\to\pi_{t-s,t-r+2}(X/\tau^{r-1})
in the abelian
1
-category
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
.

3.

The differential
d_{r}\colon E_{r}^{s,t}\to E_{r}^{s+r,t+r-1}
comes from the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s,t-r+2}(X/\tau^{r-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s-1,t+r-1}(X/\tau^{r-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s-1,t+1}(X/\tau^{r-1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\tau^{r-2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta_{r-1}^{r-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\tau^{r-2}}

through functoriality of the epi-mono factorization.

Furthermore, these assemble into a functor
E_{*}^{*,*}\colon\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathsf{SS}(\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}),X\mapsto E_{*}^{*,*}(X)
.

Remark 2.28
.

Up to reindexing, the standard SS construction in the proof of Theorem
2.26
coincides with
[
HA
, Construction 1.2.2.6]
, and the subsequent argument amounts to a reformulation of
[
HA
, Theorem 1.2.2.7]
in terms of BIPWX’s cofiber-of-
\tau
formalism.

Remark 2.29
.

Alternatively, for
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
,
\pi_{**}(X)
and
\pi_{**}(X/\tau)
form an unrolled exact couple, and the SS it induces (cf.
[
Boa99
]
) is precisely the standard SS.

Remark 2.30
.

The standard SS of
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
depends only on its completion
X_{\hat{\tau}}
since taking completion does not change any of the mod-
\tau^{r}
cofibers. On the other hand, if
X
is complete, then by the Milnor sequence and the fact
X(\infty)=0
, we see the unrolled exact couple
(\pi_{**}(X),\pi_{**}(X/\tau))
imposes
conditional convergence
to
\pi_{*}(X[\tau^{-1}])
on the standard SS in the sense of
[
Boa99
, Definition 5.10]
. We return to convergence later in Theorem
2.34
.

Remark 2.31
.

For
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
one can consider the
\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}^{\mathrm{op}}
-indexed
\tau
-Bockstein filtration in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
:

\cdots\xrightarrow{\tau}\Sigma^{0,-2}X\xrightarrow{\tau}\Sigma^{0,-1}X\xrightarrow{\tau}X\xrightarrow{\tau}\Sigma^{0,1}X\xrightarrow{\tau}\Sigma^{0,2}X\xrightarrow{\tau}\cdots

its image under
\mathrm{map}(\mathbb{1},-)\colon\mathsf{Fil}(\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp})\to\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
is the filtration
X
itself. Together with the standard SS construction, this justifies the slogan “Every SS (from filtered spectra) is a Bockstein SS”.

We can translate back and forth between
\pi_{**}(X/\tau^{r})
and the first
(r+1)
pages of the standard SS. The forward direction is clear: the standard SS is defined using the bigraded stems. The other direction is given by the
Bockstein dictionaries
4
4

4

We choose this name since the results amount to a
\tau
-Bockstein SS computation, although we will prove them in a slightly different way. Similar results in
[
BHS1
]
are referred to as the “omnibus theorem”.
below.

Theorem 2.32

(Bockstein dictionary, finite page)
.

Take
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
and
1\leqslant r<\infty
. For each
(s,t)\in\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}
, there is a finite filtration on
\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})
induced by all
\tau^{a}\colon\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X/\tau^{r-a})\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})

0=\IIm(\tau^{r})\subset\IIm(\tau^{r-1})\subset\cdots\subset\IIm(\tau^{2})\subset\IIm(\tau^{1})\subset\IIm(\tau^{0})=\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})

for which
\IIm(\tau^{a})/\IIm(\tau^{a+1})\cong Z_{r-a}^{s+a,t+a}/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
. In particular:

•

Every
x\in Z_{r}^{s,t}(X)
admits a lift
[x]\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})
. Conversely, any nonzero bigraded stem
\beta\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})
lies in some
\IIm(\tau^{a})\setminus\IIm(\tau^{a+1})
, and there is a unique nonzero
x\in Z_{r-a}^{s+a,t+a}/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
so that
\beta=\tau^{a}[x]
for a certain lift
[x]\in\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X/\tau^{r-a})
.

•

Suppose
\{x_{a,j}\}_{j\in J_{a}}
is a family of generators of
Z_{r-a}^{s+a,t+a}/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
for each
0\leqslant a\leqslant r-1
. Choose a lift
[x_{a,j}]_{\mathrm{std}}\in\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X/\tau^{r-a})
for each
x_{a,j}
. Then every
\beta\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})
can be presented as
\sum_{0\leqslant a<r}\sum_{j\in J_{a}}\tau^{a}n_{a,j}[x_{a,j}]_{\mathrm{std}}
for a certain family of integers
\{n_{a,j}\}_{0\leqslant a<r,j\in J_{a}}
.

•

Suppose
y\in Z_{c}^{s,t}
for
c\geqslant r
and
y\neq 0
until the
E_{k+1}
-page for some
k\leqslant c
. Then for every lift
[y]\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})
and any
0\leqslant a<k
the class
\tau^{a}[y]\in\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X/\tau^{r+a})
is nonzero. If furthermore we have
d_{k+1}(x)=y
, then there is a specific lift
[y]_{0}=\delta_{k}^{r}[x]\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})
for which
\tau^{k}[y]_{0}=0
, while for a generic lift
[y]
we only know that
\tau^{k}[y]\in\IIm(\tau^{k+1})\subset\pi_{t-s,t+k}(X/\tau^{r+k})
.

Proof.

The items after “in particular” follow directly from the main body. To establish the main body statement, for
0\leqslant a<r<\infty
we take the cartesian square from Lemma
2.18

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,-a}X/\tau^{r-a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,-a}X/\tau}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{a+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{a}}

and apply Lemma
2.24
.
It follows that on
\pi_{t-s,t}
we get
\IIm(\tau^{a})/\IIm(\tau^{a+1})\cong\IIm(\rho)/\IIm(\rho\delta_{a})=Z_{r-a}^{s+a,t+a}/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
as expected.
∎

Similarly, we can translate back and forth between
\pi_{**}(X)
and the
permanent cycles
in the standard SS. To phrase this we shall introduce some notions about
E_{\infty}
-page and (strong) convergence.

Definition 2.33
.

•

Suppose
E=\{E_{r}^{s,t}\}
is a SS. We denote by
Z_{\infty}^{s,t}=\lim_{r}Z_{r}^{s,t}\subset E_{2}^{s,t}
the group of
permanent cycles
, and
B_{\infty}^{s,t}=\colim_{r}B_{r}^{s,t}\subset E_{2}^{s,t}
the group of all boundaries. We refer to the collection of all quotients
E_{\infty}^{s,t}=Z_{\infty}^{s,t}/B_{\infty}^{s,t}
as the
E_{\infty}
-page
of this SS.

•

Suppose
F^{*}A=\{F^{w}\!A\}_{w\in\mathbb{Z}}
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-indexed strict
5
5

5

This means each
F^{w}A
is a subgroup of
A
.
decreasing filtration on
A\in\mathsf{Ab}
.

–

We say
F^{*}A
is
exhaustive
if
\bigcup_{w}F^{w}\!A=A
.

–

We say
F^{*}A
is
Hausdorff
if the comparison map
A\to\lim_{w}A/F^{w}\!A
is injective, where the limit is computed in
\mathsf{Ab}
. In other words,
F^{*}A
is Hausdorff if
\bigcap_{w}F^{w}\!A=0
.

–

Similarly, we say
F^{*}A
is
precomplete
6
6

6

We can treat strictly filtered abelian groups as
\tau
-torsionfree objects in
\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}[\tau]}\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Ab}
. One may wonder what is the relationship between derived
\tau
-completeness there and the notion of precompleteness above. Actually, the
\tau
-torsionfree object
F^{*}A
is derived
\tau
-complete iff it is classically
\tau
-complete, iff
F^{*}A
is both precomplete and Hausdorff. For instance, if
F^{*}A
is the constant filtration on
A=\mathbb{Z}
, then it is precomplete but not derived
\tau
-complete.
if the comparison map
A\to\lim_{w}A/F^{w}\!A
is surjective. In other words,
F^{*}A
is precomplete if
\lim^{1}_{w}F^{w}\!A=0
. We say
F^{*}A
is
complete
if it is both precomplete and Hausdorff.

•

Suppose
E=\{E_{r}^{s,t}\}
is a SS and
A=\{A_{n}\}\in\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Ab}
. We say
E

converges (strongly)
to
A
, or
E_{r}^{s,t}\Rightarrow A_{t-s}
, if for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
there is a complete exhaustive filtration
F^{*}A_{n}
together with a family of isomorphisms
F^{s}\!A_{n}/F^{s+1}\!A_{n}\cong E_{\infty}^{s,n+s}
. We also refer to
A
as the
abutment
of
E
.

Theorem 2.34

(Bockstein dictionary, infinite page)
.

Take
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
.

1.

The following strict filtration on
\pi_{t-s,t}(X)
induced by all
\tau^{a}\colon\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X)\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X)

\cdots\subset\IIm(\tau^{a})\subset\cdots\subset\IIm(\tau^{2})\subset\IIm(\tau^{1})\subset\IIm(\tau^{0})=\pi_{t-s,t}(X)

is exhaustive with associated graded
\IIm(\tau^{a})/\IIm(\tau^{a+1})\cong\IIm(\rho^{\infty}_{1})/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
, where

\IIm(\rho^{\infty}_{1}\colon\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X)\to\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X/\tau))\subset Z_{\infty}^{s+a,t+a}.

Suppose
X
is moreover complete and for each bidegree
(s,t)
the
weak obstruction

RE_{\infty}^{s,t}(X)=\lim^{1}_{r}Z_{r}^{s,t}
vanishes. Then the image of
\rho\colon\pi_{t-s,t}(X)\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)=E_{2}^{s,t}
is precisely
Z_{\infty}^{s,t}
, and this filtration is also complete, with
\IIm(\tau^{a})/\IIm(\tau^{a+1})\cong Z_{\infty}^{s+a,t+a}/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
. In particular:

•

Every
x\in Z_{\infty}^{s,t}(X)
admits a lift
[x]\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X)
. Conversely, any nonzero bigraded stem
\beta\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X)
lies in some
\IIm(\tau^{a})\setminus\IIm(\tau^{a+1})
, and there is a unique nonzero
x\in Z_{\infty}^{s+a,t+a}/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
so that
\beta=\tau^{a}[x]
for a certain lift
[x]\in\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X)
.

•

Suppose
\{x_{a,j}\}_{j\in J_{a}}
is a family of generators of
Z_{\infty}^{s+a,t+a}/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
for each
a\geqslant 0
. Choose a lift
[x_{a,j}]_{\mathrm{std}}\in\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X)
for each
x_{a,j}
. Then for any levelwise finitely supported family of integers
\{n_{a,j}\}_{a\geqslant 0,j\in J_{a}}
, the expression
\sum_{a\geqslant 0}\sum_{j\in J_{a}}\tau^{a}n_{a,j}[x_{a,j}]_{\mathrm{std}}
determines a unique element in
\pi_{t-s,t}(X)
. Conversely, every
\beta\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X)
can be presented in this form.

•

If
y\in E_{2}^{s,t}
survives to the
E_{k+1}
-page and is killed by a
d_{k+1}
-differential, then for every lift
[y]\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X)
and any
0\leqslant a<k
the class
\tau^{a}[y]\in\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X)
is nonzero. Furthermore, if
d_{k+1}(x)=y
, then there is a specific lift
[y]_{0}=\delta_{k}^{\infty}[x]\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X)
for which
\tau^{k}[y]_{0}=0
, while for a generic lift
[y]
we only know that
\tau^{k}[y]\in\IIm(\tau^{k+1})\subset\pi_{t-s,t+k}(X)
.

2.

Under the assumption of item 1, for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we further consider the filtration on
\pi_{n}(X[\tau^{-1}])

\cdots\subset\IIm(\iota_{2})\subset\IIm(\iota_{1})\subset\IIm(\iota_{0})\subset\IIm(\iota_{-1})\subset\IIm(\iota_{s})\subset\cdots

with
\IIm(\iota_{s})
being the image of
\iota_{s}\colon\pi_{n,n+s}(X)\to\pi_{n}(X[\tau^{-1}])
. This is precomplete exhaustive, so that
\IIm(\iota_{s})/\IIm(\iota_{s+1})\cong E_{\infty}^{s,n+s}
. If furthermore for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
the
whole plane obstruction

W_{n}(X)=\ker\left(\colim_{a\to-\infty}\lim_{c\to\infty}\pi_{n,a}(X/\tau^{c-a})\to\lim_{c\to\infty}\colim_{a\to-\infty}\pi_{n,a}(X/\tau^{c-a})\right)

vanishes, then these filtrations are also Hausdorff, thus
E_{r}^{s,t}(X)\Rightarrow\pi_{t-s}(X[\tau^{-1}])
. In particular:

•

Any nonzero stem
\beta\in\pi_{n}(X[\tau^{-1}])
lies in some
\IIm(\iota_{s})\setminus\IIm(\iota_{s+1})
, and there is a unique nonzero
x\in E_{\infty}^{s,n+s}
so that a certain lift
[x]\in\pi_{n,n+s}(X)
recovers
\beta
after
\tau
-inversion. In this case we say
\beta
is of filtration
s
and
x\in E_{\infty}^{s,n+s}

detects

\beta\in\pi_{n}(X[\tau^{-1}])
.

•

Suppose
\{x_{s,j}\}_{j\in J_{s}}
is a family of generators of
E_{\infty}^{s,n+s}
for each
s\in\mathbb{Z}
. Choose a lift
[x_{s,j}]_{\mathrm{std}}\in\pi_{n,n+s}(X)
for each
x_{s,j}
. Then for any
w\in\mathbb{Z}
and any levelwise finitely supported family of integers
\{k_{s,j}\}_{s\geqslant w,j\in J_{s}}
, the expression
\sum_{s\geqslant w}\sum_{j\in J_{s}}\iota_{s}(k_{s,j}[x_{s,j}]_{\mathrm{std}})
determines a unique element in
\pi_{n}(X[\tau^{-1}])
. Conversely, every
\beta\in\pi_{n}(X[\tau^{-1}])
can be presented in this form.

Proof.

The items after “in particular” follow directly from the main body. For the main body of part 1, we take the cartesian square from Lemma
2.18

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,-a}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,-a}X/\tau}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{a+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{a}}

and apply Lemma
2.24
to this square. On
\pi_{t-s,t}
we get
\IIm(\tau^{a})/\IIm(\tau^{a+1})\cong\IIm(\rho)/\IIm(\rho\delta_{a})=\IIm(\rho)/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
, here
\IIm(\rho\colon\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X)\to\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X/\tau))
is a subgroup of
Z_{\infty}^{s+a,t+a}
. It remains to prove that if
X
is complete and all
RE_{\infty}^{s,t}
vanish, then the map
\IIm(\rho)\to Z_{\infty}^{s,t}
is surjective,
\lim_{a}\IIm(\tau^{a})=0
and
\lim\nolimits^{1}_{a}\IIm(\tau^{a})=0
for each bidegree
(s,t)
. In fact, the second assertion implies the first: Suppose there is
x\in Z_{\infty}^{s,t}\subset\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau)
that does not lie in
\IIm(\rho)
, then
\delta_{1}^{\infty}(x)
is nonzero in
\pi_{t-s+1,t+1}(X)
, so
\lim_{a}\IIm(\tau^{a})=0
implies
\delta_{1}^{\infty}(x)\not\in\IIm(\tau^{a})
for
a\gg 1
, but we can take a lift
[x]\in\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{a})
and obtain the contradictory equality
\delta_{1}^{\infty}(x)=\tau^{a}\delta_{a}^{\infty}[x]
via Corollary
2.19
.

To proceed, we translate everything to the setup in
[
Boa99
]
as pointed out in Remark
2.29
. In this language,
\pi_{t-s,t}(X)
is a component of the graded abelian group
A^{s}
and
\lim_{a}\IIm(\tau^{a})_{s,t}
(resp.
\lim^{1}_{a}\IIm(\tau^{a})_{s,t}
) is a component of the graded abelian group
Q^{s}
(resp.
RQ^{s}
). Since
X
is complete, a Milnor sequence argument shows that
\lim_{s}A^{s}=0
and
\lim^{1}_{s}A^{s}=0
. If further
RE_{\infty}^{s,t}=0
for each
(s,t)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, then
Q^{s}=0
and
RQ^{s}=0
for each
s\in\mathbb{Z}
due to
[
Boa99
, Lemma 5.7 and Lemma 5.11]
. Thus, we have established the claims in part 1.

For the main body of part 2, we consider the cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-22.95573pt}{-16.31082pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right]

and apply Lemma
2.24
to this square on
\pi_{n}
. It follows that
\IIm(\iota_{s})/\IIm(\iota_{s+1})
is isomorphic to the quotient of
\IIm(\rho)=Z_{\infty}^{s,n+s}
by the image of
\pi_{n,n+s}(\fib(X\to X[\tau^{-1}]))\to\pi_{n,n+s}(X)\to\pi_{n,n+s}(X/\tau)
. Since
\fib(X\to X[\tau^{-1}])\cong\colim_{w\to{\infty}}\fib(\tau^{w}\colon X\to\Sigma^{0,w}X)\cong\colim_{w\to\infty}\Sigma^{-1,w}X/\tau^{w}
and each composite
\Sigma^{-1,w}X/\tau^{w}\to X\to X/\tau
is homotopic to
\delta_{w}
up to sign, this image can be identified with
\colim_{w\to\infty}B_{w}^{s,n+s}=B_{\infty}^{s,n+s}
. In other words,
\IIm(\iota_{s})/\IIm(\iota_{s+1})\cong Z_{\infty}^{s,n+s}/B_{\infty}^{s,n+s}=E_{\infty}^{s,n+s}
. The filtration is exhaustive as
\pi_{n}(X[\tau^{-1}])\cong\colim_{s\to\infty}\pi_{n,n+s}(X)
, which follows from compactness of
\mathbb{S}^{n,n}
. It remains to show
\lim^{1}_{s}\IIm(\iota_{s})=0
, and
\lim_{s}\IIm(\iota_{s})=0
if we further have
W_{n}=0
for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. For these we switch to the language in
[
Boa99
]
. Here each
\IIm(\iota_{s})_{n}
is a component of the graded abelian group
F^{s}
. As we already have
Q^{s}=0=RQ^{s}
for each
s
, the exact sequence in
[
Boa99
, Lemma 8.11]
shows that
\lim^{1}_{s}F^{s}=0
and
\lim_{s}F^{s}\cong W
for the graded abelian group
W
in
[
Boa99
, Lemma 8.5]
. The
n
-th component of
W
takes the stated form due to
[
HR19
, Theorem 7.5]
, which concludes part 2.
∎

Remark 2.35
.

•

For
(s,t)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, the weak obstruction
RE_{\infty}^{s,t}=0
if the family
\{Z_{r}^{s,t}\}_{r\geqslant 1}
is pro-constant, in particular if there exists some
K\gg 1
so that
Z_{r}^{s,t}=Z_{K}^{s,t}
for all
r\geqslant K
(i.e. there are only finitely many pages for which there exists a nontrivial differential out of bidegree
(s,t)
).

•

For
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the whole plane obstruction
W_{n}=0
if there exists
K\gg 1
so that for any
s_{1},s_{2}>K
,
d_{s_{1}+s_{2}}\colon E_{s_{1}+s_{2}}^{-s_{2},n+1-s_{2}}\to E_{s_{1}+s_{2}}^{s_{1},n+s_{1}}
is the zero map (i.e. there does not exist an infinite family of nontrivial pairwise crossing differentials with increasing length hitting stem
n
), cf.
[
Boa99
, Lemma 8.1]
,
[
HR19
, Proposition 5.3 and Remark 5.4]
.

Summary 2.36
.

We pause to summarize the behavior of
\tau^{k}
,
\rho
, and
\delta_{k}
in terms of the standard SS.

•

The bigraded homotopy group
\pi_{n,n+s}(X/\tau^{r})
is built out of
r
layers:

–

the
0
th layer consists of classes
[x]
coming from
Z_{r}
-cycles in bidegree
(s,n+s)
;

–

the
1
st layer consists of classes
\tau[x]
coming from
Z_{r-1}
-cycles in bidegree
(s+1,n+s+1)
, modulo the
d_{2}
-boundaries;

–

the
2
nd layer consists of classes
\tau^{2}[x]
coming from
Z_{r-2}
-cycles in bidegree
(s+2,n+s+2)
, modulo the
d_{3}
-boundaries;

–

\cdots

–

the
(r-1)
st layer consists of classes
\tau^{r-1}[x]
coming from
Z_{1}
-cycles
=E_{2}
-classes in bidegree
(s+r-1,n+s+r-1)
, modulo the
d_{r}
-boundaries.

•

Multiplication by
\tau^{k}
raises the layer by
k
and kills
k
additional pages of boundaries.

•

The projection operator
\rho^{r}_{k}
removes all layers of order at least
k
.

•

The operator
\delta_{k}^{r}\colon\pi_{**}(X/\tau^{k})\to\pi_{**}(X/\tau^{r})
behaves like a
total differential
combining information from
d_{2}
through
d_{k+r}
, in the sense that
\delta_{k}^{r}\tau^{a}[x]=\tau^{b}[y]
if and only if
d_{k-a+b+1}(x)=y
. This is to be made precise below in Theorem
2.38
.

Lemma 2.37
.

Suppose
\mathcal{C}
is a stable
\infty
-category. For any cospan
A\to C\leftarrow B
in
\mathcal{C}
and any homological functor
F\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{A}
, the limit comparison map
F(A\times_{C}B)\to F(A)\times_{F(C)}F(B)
is surjective, and its kernel is given by the cokernel of
F(\Sigma^{-1}A)\oplus F(\Sigma^{-1}B)\to F(\Sigma^{-1}C)
.

Proof.

The fiber sequence
A\times_{C}B\to A\oplus B\to C
leads to a long exact sequence

\cdots\to F(\Sigma^{-1}A)\oplus F(\Sigma^{-1}B)\to F(\Sigma^{-1}C)\to F(A\times_{C}B)\to F(A)\oplus F(B)\to F(C)\to\cdots

The desired result follows by breaking this into short exact sequences.
∎

Theorem 2.38
.

For
1\leqslant k<\infty
and
1\leqslant r\leqslant\infty
, consider
\delta_{k}^{r}\colon\pi_{n+1,n+s+1}(X/\tau^{k})\to\pi_{n,n+s+k}(X/\tau^{r})
.

•

Suppose
0\leqslant a<k
,
0\leqslant b<r
,
x\in E_{2}^{s,n+s+1}(X)
and
y\in E_{2}^{s+k-a+b+1,n+s+k-a+b+1}(X)
. Then there exist lifts
[x]\in\pi_{n+1,n+s+1}(X/\tau^{k-a}),[y]\in\pi_{n,n+s+k-a+b+1}(X/\tau^{r-b})
with
\delta_{k}^{r}\tau^{a}[x]=\tau^{b}[y]
if and only if
x,y
are both
(k-a+b)
-cycles and
d_{k-a+b+1}(x)=y
in the standard SS.

•

Suppose
0\leqslant a<k
and
x\in E_{2}^{s,n+s+1}(X)
. For
r<\infty
, there exists a lift
[x]\in\pi_{n+1,n+s+1}(X/\tau^{k-a})
such that
\delta_{k}^{r}\tau^{a}[x]=0
if and only if
x
is an
(k-a+r)
-cycle. For
r=\infty
, the “only if” direction also holds true, while the “if” direction is true provided that
X
is complete and all weak obstructions
RE_{\infty}^{s,t}
(cf. Theorem
2.34
) vanish.

Proof.

For the “only if” direction in the first item, observe that
\delta_{k}^{r}\tau^{a}[x]=\delta_{k-a}^{r}[x]
by Corollary
2.19
, and
\delta_{k-a}^{r}[x]=\tau^{b}[y]
implies by Lemma
2.20
and Lemma
2.37
that there is a lift
[x]^{\prime}\in\pi_{n+1,n+s+1}(X/\tau^{k-a+b})
refining
[x]
with
\delta_{k-a+b}^{1}[x]^{\prime}=y
, thus
d_{k-a+b+1}(x)=y
in the standard spectral sequence. For the “if” direction,
d_{k-a+b+1}(x)=y
means there is a lift
[x]_{1}\in\pi_{n+1,n+s+1}(X/\tau^{k-a+b})
so that
\delta_{k-a+b}[x]_{1}=y_{1}
, with
y-y_{1}\in B_{k-a+b}^{s+k-a+b+1,n+s+k-a+b+1}
. By construction,

\delta_{k-a+b-1}^{1}\colon\pi_{n+1,n+s+2}(X/\tau^{k-a+b-1})\to B_{k-a+b}^{s+k-a+b+1,n+s+k-a+b+1}

is surjective, so we can find
[z]\in\pi_{n+1,n+s+2}(X/\tau^{k-a+b-1})
with
\delta_{k-a+b-1}^{1}[z]=y-y_{1}
. Thus, taking
[x]=[x]_{1}+\tau[z]
, we have
\delta^{1}_{k-a+b}[x]=y
on the
E_{2}
-page, therefore
[y]=\delta_{k-a+b}^{r-b}[x]
is a lift of
y
for which
\tau^{b}[y]=\tau^{b}\delta_{k-a+b}^{r-b}[x]=\delta_{k-a}^{r}[x]=\delta_{k}^{r}\tau^{a}[x]
by reversing the argument for the “only if” direction. The second item with
r<\infty
follows from the first item applied to
a,b=r-1,x,y=0\in E_{2}^{s+k-a+r,n+s+k-a+r}
. For
r=\infty
, the “only if” direction follows from the
r<\infty
cases, while the “if” direction holds true as
x\in Z_{\infty}^{s,n+s+1}
together with completeness and vanishing weak obstructions yield the existence of a lift
[x]^{\prime}\in\pi_{n+1,n+s+1}(X)
for which
[x]=\rho_{k-a}^{\infty}[x]^{\prime}
satisfies
\delta_{k}^{\infty}\tau^{a}[x]=\delta_{k-a}^{\infty}[x]=0
.
∎

Remark 2.39
.

A differential
d_{r}(x)=y
is
essential
if
y\neq 0
on the
E_{r}
-page. In the language of total differential
\delta
above,
d_{k-a+b+1}(x)=y
is essential iff the lift
\delta_{k}\tau^{a}[x]=\tau^{b}[y]\not\in\IIm(\tau^{b+1})
.

It is also worthwhile to single out the “limit case” behavior of the fundamental distinguished triangle, which appears in the proof of Theorem
2.34
. Heuristically, this serves as the unification of all possible differentials in the standard SS of a filtered spectrum
X
.

Construction 2.40
.

For each
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
, consider the tensor product of
X
with the octahedral distinguished triangle coming from the colimit of all consecutive maps

0\to\mathbb{1}\xrightarrow{\tau^{k}}\Sigma^{0,k}\mathbb{1}

as
k\in\mathbb{N}
increases monotonically to
\infty
. The resulting triangle takes the form

X\xrightarrow{\iota\,=\,\langle\tau^{\infty}\rangle}X[\tau^{-1}]\xrightarrow{\rho_{\infty}^{\infty}}\Sigma^{1,0}X^{\tau\textup{-tors}}=\colim_{k\to\infty}\Sigma^{0,k}X/\tau^{k}\xrightarrow{\delta_{\infty}^{\infty}}\Sigma^{1,0}X

where
X[\tau^{-1}]=\colim_{k\to{\infty}}\Sigma^{0,k}X
, and
X^{\tau\textup{-tors}}=\colim_{k\to\infty}\Sigma^{-1,k}X/\tau^{k}
is the
\tau
-torsionization
of
X
abstractly constructed as the value of
X
under the left adjoint of the completion functor
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp},X\mapsto X_{\hat{\tau}}
. We may also describe the three maps in explicit terms:

•

The first map
\iota=\langle\tau^{\infty}\rangle
is the
\tau
-inversion map, i.e. the colimit of all maps
\tau^{k}\colon X\to\Sigma^{0,k}X
. Concretely, for any permanent cycle
x
, it takes
[x]\in\pi_{**}(X)
to the stem it detects in the abutment
\pi_{*}(X[\tau^{-1}])
.

•

The second map
\rho_{\infty}^{\infty}
is the colimit of all projections
\rho^{\infty}_{k}\colon\Sigma^{0,k}X\to\Sigma^{0,k}X/\tau^{k}
. Concretely, in good cases each
\alpha\in\pi_{*}(X[\tau^{-1}])
comes from a surviving permanent cycle
x
of filtration
\geqslant-k
for some
k\geqslant 0
in that a certain lift
[x]
detects
\alpha
, while
\rho^{\infty}_{\infty}(\alpha)
amounts to forgetting
x
to a
k
-cycle.

•

The third map
\delta^{\infty}_{\infty}
is the
\tau
-torsionization map, i.e. the colimit of all maps
\delta_{k}^{\infty}\colon\Sigma^{0,k}X/\tau^{k}\to\Sigma^{1,0}X
. Concretely, it takes every class
[x]\in\pi_{**}(X/\tau^{k})\hookrightarrow\pi_{**}(X^{\tau\textup{-tors}})
to the total differential
\delta_{k}^{\infty}[x]
, which is independent of the choice of
k
due to Corollary
2.19
.

We wrap up this subsection by generalizing some of the results above to the situation where
\mathsf{Sp}
is replaced by an arbitrary
t
-
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
, in terms of the
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}\!
-valued standard spectral sequence from Corollary
2.27
.

Corollary 2.41
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable stable
\infty
-category,
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
be an abelian
1
-category and
\pi_{0}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
be a homological functor. Take
X\in\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})
.

•

For each
r\in\mathbb{N},s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
with
r\geqslant 1
, there is a finite filtration on
\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
induced by all maps in
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
of the form
\tau^{a}\colon\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X/\tau^{r-a})\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})

0=\IIm(\tau^{r})\subset\IIm(\tau^{r-1})\subset\cdots\subset\IIm(\tau^{2})\subset\IIm(\tau^{1})\subset\IIm(\tau^{0})=\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{r})

with
\IIm(\tau^{a})/\IIm(\tau^{a+1})\cong Z_{r-a}^{s+a,t+a}/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
. This follows by the same proof as in Theorem
2.32
.

•

For each
r,k,a,b\in\mathbb{N},n,s\in\mathbb{Z}
with
a<k,b<r
, suppose there is an object
T\in\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
with two maps
x\colon T\to E_{2}^{s,n+s+1},y\colon T\to E_{2}^{s+k-a+b+1,n+s+k-a+b+1}
. Then the following are equivalent:

–

There exist
[x]\colon T\to\pi_{n+1,n+s+1}(X/\tau^{k-a})
and
[y]\colon T\to\pi_{n,n+s+k-a+b+1}(X/\tau^{r-b})
such that
\rho^{k-a}_{1}[x]=x,\rho^{r-b}_{1}[y]=y
and
\delta_{k}^{r}\tau^{a}[x]=\tau^{b}[y]
as maps in
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
.

–

The maps
x,y
lift (uniquely) to
x\colon T\to Z_{k-a+b}^{s,n+s+1}
,
y\colon T\to Z_{k-a+b}^{s+k-a+b+1,n+s+k-a+b+1}
, so that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{k-a+b}^{s,n+s+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{k-a+b+1}^{s,n+s+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{k-a+b}^{s+k-a+b+1,n+s+k-a+b+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{k-a+b+1}^{s+k-a+b+1,n+s+k-a+b+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{k-a+b+1}}

is a commutative square in
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
.

This follows by the same proof as in Theorem
2.38
.

•

On the other hand, the
r=\infty
case of our Bockstein dictionary does not have a direct generalization. Indeed, suppose
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
admits sequential limits, exact sequential colimits, and the functor
\pi_{0}
preserves sequential colimits. Then for each
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
, we can still construct the exhaustive strict filtration
on
\pi_{t-s,t}(X)

\cdots\subset\IIm(\tau^{a})\subset\cdots\subset\IIm(\tau^{2})\subset\IIm(\tau^{1})\subset\IIm(\tau^{0})=\pi_{t-s,t}(X)

induced by all maps in
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
of the form
\tau^{a}\colon\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X)\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X)
, while the identification
\IIm(\tau^{a})/\IIm(\tau^{a+1})\cong\IIm(\rho^{\infty}_{1})/B_{a+1}^{s+a,t+a}
with

\IIm(\rho^{\infty}_{1}\colon\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X)\to\pi_{t-s,t+a}(X/\tau))\subset Z_{\infty}^{s+a,t+a}

still holds true due to the same proof as in Theorem
2.34
. However, to further study the completeness of this filtration, and subsequently the strong convergence property of the
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}\!
-valued standard SS, would require a detailed analysis of certain obstruction classes (
RE^{s,t}_{\infty},W_{n}
, etc.) coming from the higher derived functors of the sequential limit functor in
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
. In the case
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}=\mathsf{Ab}
, all higher derived limits vanish except for
\lim^{1}
, and the required analysis is achieved in
[
Boa99
]
. For general
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}
, however, the contributions from
\lim^{2}
, etc. introduce substantial additional obstructions, and we do not pursue the corresponding analysis here.

Remark 2.42
.

We end this subsection with some historical comments and attributions concerning the Bockstein dictionary results proved above.

•

Historically, people usually define the standard SS of a filtered spectrum via approaches that do not involve bigraded stems, e.g. the exact couple formalism in
[
Boa99
]
. Consequently, they tend to understand the bigraded stems of
X
(or
X/\tau^{r}
) as the abutment of the trigraded
\tau
-Bockstein SS associated to the
\tau
-filtration on the object
X
(or
X/\tau^{r}
) in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
. In this way,

(a).

The content of Theorem
2.34
becomes that the trigraded
\tau
-Bockstein SS of
X
is
rigid
, i.e. it admits the action of a formal parameter
\tau
and is completely determined by
E_{*}^{*,*}(X)=E_{*}^{*,*,*}(X)[\tau^{-1}]
. See Theorem
A.6
item 1.

(b).

The content of Theorem
2.32
becomes that the trigraded
\tau
-Bockstein SS of
X/\tau^{r}
is
rigid
, or more precisely the translation rule between this SS and the standard SS
E_{*}^{*,*}(X)
. See Theorem
A.6
item 3.

Actually, as we will discuss in Appendix
A
, all examples of rigid trigraded SS come from
\tau
-Bockstein filtrations, so the history of the rigidity results for SS is the same as that of the Bockstein dictionary type results for filtered objects.

•

The first rigidity result of type (a) is due to Hu–Kriz–Ormsby
[
Hu-Kriz-Ormsby
, Lemma 15]
, which proves the rigidity of motivic Adams–Novikov SS (mANSS) of
\mathbb{1}\in\mathsf{SH}(\mathbb{C})
, i.e. the complete translation between data in this mANSS and the data in the classical ANSS of
\mathbb{S}^{0}
.

•

After this, Isaksen
[
Isaksen-stable-stems
, Proposition 6.2.5]
proves the rigidity of the mANSS of
\mathbb{1}/\tau\in\mathsf{SH}(\mathbb{C})
, i.e. this SS has no nontrivial differential and the abutment
\pi_{**}(\mathbb{1}/\tau)
is the classical ANSS
E_{2}
-page of
\mathbb{S}^{0}
. This is the first rigidity result of type (b).

•

Burklund–Hahn–Senger
[
BHS1
, Theorem A.8]
proves the rigidity of
E
-synthetic ASS of
\nu X
. This generalizes the
\mathbb{C}
-motivic rigidity results since Pstragowski
[
Pst23
, Theorem 7.34]
identifies the
\infty
-category of (
p
-complete cellular)
\mathbb{C}
-motivic spectra with the full sub-
\infty
-category of (
p
-complete)
\mathrm{MU}
-synthetic spectra
\mathsf{Syn}_{\mathrm{MU}}
generated under colimits by the even bigraded spheres

\{\mathbb{S}^{n,2w}\}_{n,w\in\mathbb{Z}}
.

Furthermore, they convert (for the first time) the rigidity result into a type (a) Bockstein dictionary statement for bigraded stems
[
BHS1
, Theorem A.1]
, under the name of the “omnibus theorem”. They also briefly mention the truncated case (i.e.
E
-synthetic ASS of
\nu X/\tau^{r}
) in
[
BHS1
, Corollary A.11]
.

•

Burklund–Xu
[
Burklund-Xu
, Theorem 2.8]
proves the rigidity of the motivic Cartan–Eilenberg SS.

•

Carrick–Davies–van Nigtevecht
[
CDvN2
, § 2]
,
[
vN25
, § 3]
establish the “omnibus theorem” and its truncated variant in filtered spectra by adapting the
\tau
-Bockstein SS argument in
[
BHS1
]
to
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
. The results they obtain, namely
[
vN25
, Theorem 3.62, Theorem 3.67 and Theorem 3.70]
, are essentially equivalent to our Theorem
2.32
and Theorem
2.34
.

Note that the present paper approaches these results from a different perspective. Rather than treating the bigraded stems of
X
and
X/\tau^{r}
as auxiliary tools for understanding the standard SS of
X
, we regard them as part of the foundational data of the standard SS itself. Accordingly, our proof of the Bockstein dictionary results does not use spectral sequence arguments, but reduces instead to a simple lemma on homological functors, Lemma
2.24
.

In the rigidity story, one remaining missing piece is the identification of the synthetic Adams SS of
\nu X/\tau^{r}
with a truncated variant of the trigraded
\tau
-Bockstein SS. In Appendix
A
, we give a more extensive review of the rigidity results and fill this gap in Theorem
A.9
.

2.3   Multiplicativity

Next we discuss the multiplicativity of the standard SS. Recall that
\mathbb{1}/\tau
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
.

Construction 2.43

(Accelerations)
.

Take
k\geqslant 1
.

•

The map of abelian groups
\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z},t\mapsto kt
is compatible with the poset structures, so it left Kan extends to a symmetric monoidal left adjoint
\mathrm{Sl}_{k}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
that sends
\mathbb{S}^{n,w}
to
\mathbb{S}^{n,kw}
. For general
X
,
\mathrm{Sl}_{k}(X)(w)=X(\lceil w/k\rceil)
, so we refer to it as “
k
-fold slowdown
” of the filtration
X
. Concretely,
\pi_{n,w}(\mathrm{Sl}_{k}X)=\pi_{n,\lfloor w/k\rfloor}X
. Note that
\mathrm{Sl}_{k}
sends
\tau\colon\mathbb{S}^{0,-1}\to\mathbb{S}^{0,0}
to
\tau^{k}\colon\mathbb{S}^{0,-k}\to\mathbb{S}^{0,0}
, so
\mathrm{Sl}_{k}(\mathbb{1}/\tau)=\mathbb{1}/\tau^{k}
also admits an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra structure.

•

The corresponding right adjoint
\mathrm{Ac}_{k}
(which is automatically lax symmetric monoidal) sends
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
to
\mathrm{Ac}_{k}X
with
(\mathrm{Ac}_{k}X)(w)=\mathrm{map}(\mathbb{S}^{0,w},\mathrm{Ac}_{k}X)=\mathrm{map}(\mathrm{Sl}_{k}(\mathbb{S}^{0,w}),X)=X(kw)
, so it amounts to a “
k
-fold acceleration
” of the filtration
X
. Concretely,
\pi_{n,w}(\mathrm{Ac}_{k}X)=\pi_{n,kw}X
. It follows that
\mathrm{Ac}_{k}(\mathbb{1}/\tau^{k})\cong\mathbb{1}/\tau
and
\mathrm{Ac}_{k}(X/\tau^{k})\cong(\mathrm{Ac}_{k}X)/\tau
as
\mathbb{1}/\tau
-modules for each
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
. Furthermore, the image of the total differential
\delta_{k}^{k}\colon X/\tau^{k}\to\Sigma^{1,-k}X/\tau^{k}
under
\mathrm{Ac}_{k}
turns out to be
\delta_{1}^{1}
on
(\mathrm{Ac}_{k}X)/\tau
.

Remark 2.44
.

Both
\mathbb{1}
and
\mathbb{1}/\tau
lie in the full subcategory
\mathsf{Fil}_{\leqslant 0}\mathsf{Sp}\subset\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
generated under colimits by
\{\mathbb{S}^{n,w}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z},w\leqslant 0}
, for which we have an identification
\mathsf{Fil}_{\leqslant 0}\mathsf{Sp}\cong\mathsf{Fun}(\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}^{\mathrm{op}}_{\leqslant 0},\mathsf{Sp})
where
\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}_{\leqslant 0}
is the poset of nonpositive integers. We can reproduce the construction of
\mathrm{Sl}_{k}
(and
\mathrm{Ac}_{k}
) in
\mathsf{Fil}_{\leqslant 0}\mathsf{Sp}
, which leads to a commutative square of symmetric monoidal left adjoints

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Fil}_{\leqslant 0}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Fil}_{\leqslant 0}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sl}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{incl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{incl}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sl}_{k}}

while we have an extra advantage in
\mathsf{Fil}_{\leqslant 0}\mathsf{Sp}
: among the symmetric monoidal functors (i.e. maps of posets)
\cdot k\colon\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}_{\leqslant 0}\to\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}_{\leqslant 0}
there are symmetric monoidal natural transformations
\cdot(k+1)\to\cdot{k}
for all
k\geqslant 1
, and they extend to symmetric monoidal transformations
\mathrm{Sl}_{k+1}\to\mathrm{Sl}_{k}
by the universal property of spectral presheaves. This refines the diagram

\cdots\xrightarrow{\rho^{5}_{4}}\mathrm{Sl}_{4}(\mathbb{1}/\tau)=\mathbb{1}/\tau^{4}\xrightarrow{\rho^{4}_{3}}\mathrm{Sl}_{3}(\mathbb{1}/\tau)=\mathbb{1}/\tau^{3}\xrightarrow{\rho^{3}_{2}}\mathrm{Sl}_{2}(\mathbb{1}/\tau)=\mathbb{1}/\tau^{2}\xrightarrow{\rho^{2}_{1}}\mathrm{Sl}_{1}(\mathbb{1}/\tau)=\mathbb{1}/\tau

to a sequence of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring maps, whose limit recovers
\mathbb{1}=\mathbb{1}_{\hat{\tau}}
as an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra.

Recollection 2.45

(Koszul sign rule)
.

Take
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geqslant 1}\cup\{\infty\}
.

•

Suppose
\mathcal{A}
is an additively
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal
1
-category. Then there is a
Koszul
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal structure
on
\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{A})
. For
n=1
this coincides with the Day convolution monoidal structure. In general, the difference between
\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{A})^{\mathrm{Day}}
and
\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{A})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
lies solely in the swap map. As every object
X=\{X^{w}\}_{w\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{A})
is the direct sum of each
X^{w}[w]
, the object
X^{w}\in\mathcal{A}
concentrated in degree
w
, it suffices to treat the swap map intertwining
X[m_{1}]
and
Y[m_{2}]
for
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\delta}
and
X,Y\in\mathcal{A}
. In fact, if
\epsilon=\epsilon_{X,Y}\colon X\otimes Y\to Y\otimes X
is the swap map in
\mathcal{A}
, then

\epsilon[m_{1}+m_{2}]\colon X[m_{1}]\otimes Y[m_{2}]=(X\otimes Y)[m_{1}+m_{2}]\to(Y\otimes X)[m_{1}+m_{2}]=Y[m_{2}]\otimes X[m_{1}]

is the swap map intertwining
X[m_{1}]
and
Y[m_{2}]
in
\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{A})^{\mathrm{Day}}
, while

(-1)^{m_{1}m_{2}}\epsilon[m_{1}+m_{2}]\colon X[m_{1}]\otimes Y[m_{2}]\to Y[m_{2}]\otimes X[m_{1}]

is the swap map intertwining
X[m_{1}]
and
Y[m_{2}]
in
\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{A})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
.

•

There is a unique
Koszul
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal structure
on
\mathsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})
so that the forgetful functor
U\colon\mathsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{A}),(\{M^{s}\}_{s\in\mathbb{Z}},d\colon M^{s}\to M^{s+1})\mapsto\{M^{s}\}_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}
is
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal with Koszul
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal structure on the target. Concretely,

–

For
M,N\in\mathsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})
, the tensor product
M\otimes N
is given on the underlying object by
(M\otimes N)^{s}=\bigoplus_{p+q=s}M^{p}\otimes N^{q}
, while the differential
d\colon(M\otimes N)^{s}\to(M\otimes N)^{s+1}
is the sum of maps
d\otimes\mathrm{id}+(-1)^{p}\mathrm{id}\otimes d\colon M^{p}\otimes N^{q}\to M^{p+1}\otimes N^{q}\oplus M^{p}\otimes N^{q+1}
.

–

In the case
n\geqslant 2
, for each pair
M,N\in\mathsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})
, the swap map on the underlying object
\epsilon\colon U(M)\otimes U(N)\to U(N)\otimes U(M)
is automatically compatible with differentials on both sides, so these assemble into an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal structure
\mathsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
.

Also, taking cycles/homology yields lax
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal functors
Z,H\colon\mathsf{Ch}(\mathcal{A})^{\mathrm{Kos}}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{A})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
.

Recollection 2.46
.

There is a
Beilinson
t
-structure
on the
\infty
-category
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
, in which

•

\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{B}_{\geqslant 0}
consists of filtrations
X
so that each
(X/\tau)(-w)
is
w
-connective.

•

\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{B}_{\leqslant 0}
consists of complete filtrations
X
so that each
(X/\tau)(-w)
is
w
-coconnective.

Its heart
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{B,\heartsuit}
can be identified with
\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Ab})
, the
1
-category of chain complexes of abelian groups,
and the Day convolution symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
induces the Koszul symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Ab})
, cf.
[
Ant24
, § 3 and § 8]
.
For each
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp},n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
n
-th homotopy object
\pi^{B}_{n}X\in\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Ab})
is the chain complex
(\pi_{n-a,a}(X/\tau),\delta_{1}^{1}\colon\pi_{n-a,a}(X/\tau)\to\pi_{n-a-1,a+1}(X/\tau))
. Thus, the collection
\{\pi_{n}^{B}(X)\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
amounts to the
E_{2}
-page of the standard SS together with its
d_{2}
differentials.

Proposition 2.47
.

Take
n\in\mathbb{N}_{\geqslant 1}\cup\{\infty\}
. Suppose
\mathcal{E}
is a stably
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal
t
-
\infty
-category such that

•

The
\infty
-category
\mathcal{E}
admits sequential colimits.

•

The tensor product on
\mathcal{E}
is exact and preserves sequential colimits in each variable.

•

The
t
-structure on
\mathcal{E}
is compatible with the tensor product (cf.
[
HA
, Example 2.2.1.3]
).

Then the functor
\pi_{*}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit})
upgrades to a lax
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal functor
\pi_{*}\colon\mathcal{E}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
.

Proof.

This is
[
Ant24
, Theorem 8.7]
. Roughly speaking, there is a
diagonal
t
-structure
on
\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})
whose connective part
\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})^{D}_{\geqslant 0}
(resp. whose coconnective part
\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})^{D}_{\leqslant 0}
) consists of filtrations
X
so that each
X(w)
is
w
-connective (resp.
w
-coconnective) in
\mathcal{E}
, and its heart
\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})^{D,\heartsuit}
is equivalent to
\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
as an
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidal
1
-category. The functor we desire is the composite

\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{const}}\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})\xrightarrow{\pi_{0}^{D}}\mathsf{Fil}(\mathcal{E})^{D,\heartsuit}\cong\mathsf{Gr}(\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit})^{\mathrm{Kos}}

whose lax
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-monoidality follows from
[
HA
, Example 2.2.1.10]
.
∎

Example 2.48
.

•

Taking
\mathcal{E}=\mathsf{Sp}
, we obtain a lax symmetric monoidal functor
\pi_{*}\colon\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
. As the functor
i^{*}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}
induced by precomposing
i\colon\mathbb{Z}^{\delta}\to\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}
is also lax symmetric monoidal, we obtain a lax symmetric monoidal composite

\pi_{**}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Day}}\cong\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Day}})^{\mathrm{Kos}},\quad X\mapsto\{\pi_{n,w}(X)\}_{n,w\in\mathbb{Z}}.

Here for bigraded objects such as
\{\pi_{n,w}(X)\}_{n,w\in\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Day}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
, we always use the first index (in this example:
n
) to denote the external (in this example: Koszul) grading and use the second index (in this example:
w
) to denote the internal (in this example: Day) grading.

•

Taking
\mathcal{E}=\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
with its Beilinson
t
-structure, we deduce that

E_{2}=\pi^{B}_{*}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Kos}},\quad X\mapsto(\{\pi_{n-w,w}(X/\tau)=E_{2}^{2w-n,w}(X)\}_{n,w\in\mathbb{Z}},d_{2})

is lax symmetric monoidal. Also, the functor
E_{2}\mathrm{Ac}_{k}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
is lax symmetric monoidal for each
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\geqslant 1
.

Lemma 2.49
.

There is a commutative square in
\mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Cat})^{\mathrm{lax}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Day}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes\mathbb{1}/\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{2}=\pi^{B}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{**}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{forget}}

in which the symmetric monoidal equivalence
v^{*}\colon\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Day}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}\cong\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
is given by precomposing the isomorphism

v\colon\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}\times\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}\to\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}\times\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}},(n,a)\mapsto(n-a,a)
.

Proof.

To prove this we introduce two
t
-structures on
\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}
:

•

The
pointwise
t
-structure
, whose connective part
\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}^{P}_{\geqslant 0}
(resp. coconnective part
\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}^{P}_{\leqslant 0}
) consists of
X\in\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}
such that each
X(w)
is
0
-connective (resp.
0
-coconnective). Its heart
\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}^{P,\heartsuit}
is equivalent to
\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Day}}
as a symmetric monoidal
1
-category.

•

The
Beilinson
t
-structure
, whose connective part
\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}^{B}_{\geqslant 0}
(resp. coconnective part
\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}^{B}_{\leqslant 0}
) consists of
X\in\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}
such that each
X(-w)
is
w
-connective (resp.
w
-coconnective). Its heart
\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}^{B,\heartsuit}
is equivalent to
\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
as a symmetric monoidal
1
-category.

The symmetric monoidal functor
-/\tau\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}
is
t
-exact for Beilinson
t
-structures on both sides, so it induces a commutative square in
\mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Cat})^{\mathrm{lax}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{2}=\pi_{*}^{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-/\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{forget}}

On the other hand, the composite
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\xrightarrow{-\otimes\mathbb{1}/\tau}\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\xrightarrow{i^{*}}\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}
can be identified with
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\xrightarrow{-/\tau}\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}
, and
\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Day}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
can be identified with
\pi_{*}^{P}\colon\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{P,\heartsuit})^{\mathrm{Kos}}\cong\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Day}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
by examining the construction in the proof of Proposition
2.47
. What remains is to establish the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Day}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{B}}

in
\mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Cat})^{\mathrm{lax}}
. Actually, as each arrow of this tentative square is already symmetric monoidal and the lower-right corner is a
1
-category, it suffices to fill the square in
\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Cat})^{\mathrm{lax}}
, where
(-)^{\mathrm{Kos}}=(-)^{\mathrm{Day}}
.
We achieve this by pasting together two squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{B}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{P}=\pi_{**}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}^{P}=\pi_{**}}

Here, the LHS square is induced by the monoidal equivalence
V\colon\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp},\{X(w)\}_{w\in\mathbb{Z}}\mapsto\{\Sigma^{-w}X(w)\}_{w\in\mathbb{Z}}
, which is
t
-exact for the pointwise
t
-structure on the source and the Beilinson
t
-structure on the target. For the RHS square, note that the lax monoidal functor
\pi^{P}_{*}=\pi_{**}\colon\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab}))
admits an alternative description as the composite

\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}\xrightarrow{\kappa}\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\pi_{0}))}\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab}))

in which
\kappa
is the monoidal left adjoint sending
\{X(w)\}_{w\in\mathbb{Z}}
to
\{\Sigma^{n}X(w)\}_{n,w\in\mathbb{Z}}
. The RHS square is then deduced from the square of monoidal left adjoints

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\kappa}

whose existence follows from the monoidal universal property of
\mathsf{Gr}\mathsf{Sp}
.
∎

Theorem 2.50
.

Take a map
F\colon X\otimes Y\to T
in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
. For any
k\geqslant 1
, the bilinear pairing

F=F/\tau^{k}\colon\pi_{n_{1},w_{1}}(X/\tau^{k})\times\pi_{n_{2},w_{2}}(Y/\tau^{k})\to\pi_{n_{1}+n_{2},w_{1}+w_{2}}(T/\tau^{k})

extracted from the lax monoidality of
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\xrightarrow{-\otimes\mathbb{1}/\tau^{k}}\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\xrightarrow{\pi_{**}}\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Day}}
satisfies the Leibniz rule for total differentials
\delta_{k}=\delta_{k}^{k}
, in the sense that

\delta_{k}F(\alpha,\beta)=F(\delta_{k}(\alpha),\beta)+(-1)^{n_{1}}F(\alpha,\delta_{k}(\beta)).

In particular, this leads to a bilinear map
F\colon E_{k+1}^{s_{1},t_{1}}(X)\times E_{k+1}^{s_{2},t_{2}}(Y)\to E_{k+1}^{s_{1}+s_{2},t_{1}+t_{2}}(T)
satisfying the classical Leibniz rule, in the sense that
d_{k+1}F(x,y)=F(d_{k+1}(x),y)+(-1)^{t_{1}-s_{1}}F(x,d_{k+1}(y))
.

Proof.

For
k=1
both claims follow from the lax monoidality of
E_{2}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ch}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Kos}}
together with Lemma
2.49
. In general, we work with the composite
E_{2}\mathrm{Ac}_{k}
. By replacing
F\colon X\otimes Y\to T
with
F\colon\Sigma^{0,w_{1}}X\otimes\Sigma^{0,w_{2}}Y\to\Sigma^{0,w_{1}+w_{2}}T
(this does not cause any sign issue as the swap map on
\mathbb{S}^{0,1}\otimes\mathbb{S}^{0,1}
is homotopic to the identity, which is already true in
\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}
), we can assume that
w_{1}=w_{2}=0
. Note that
\pi_{n,0}(X/\tau^{k})=\pi_{n,0}(\mathrm{Ac}_{k}(X/\tau^{k}))=\pi_{n,0}(\mathrm{Ac}_{k}(X)/\tau)
and similarly for
Y
, while
\delta_{k}
becomes
\delta_{1}
after taking
\mathrm{Ac}_{k}
. Therefore, the image of
F\colon X\otimes Y\to T
under the lax monoidal functor
E_{2}\mathrm{Ac}_{k}
gives rise to a refinement of the bilinear pairing on mod
\tau^{k}
stems in this case, with Leibniz rule for total differentials as expected. To construct the pairing on the
E_{k+1}
-page, recall in the proof of Theorem
2.26
we identify
E_{k+1}^{s,t}(X)
with the image of
\cdot\tau^{k-1}\colon\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{k})\to\pi_{t-s,t-k+1}(X/\tau^{k})
. From this, we get the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{k}\otimes Y/\tau^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T/\tau^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,k-1}(X/\tau^{k}\otimes Y/\tau^{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{0,k-1}T/\tau^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\tau^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\tau^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

which further induces the diagram below after taking
\pi_{t-s,t}
(with
s=s_{1}+s_{2},t=t_{1}+t_{2}
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t_{1}-s_{1},t_{1}}(X/\tau^{k})\otimes_{\mathsf{Ab}}\pi_{t_{2}-s_{2},t_{2}}(Y/\tau^{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{k}\otimes Y/\tau^{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s,t}(T/\tau^{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s,t-k+1}(X/\tau^{k}\otimes Y/\tau^{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{t-s,t-k+1}(T/\tau^{k})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\tau^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\tau^{k-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

The composite for the arrows on the left
\pi_{t_{1}-s_{1},t_{1}}(X/\tau^{k})\otimes_{\mathsf{Ab}}\pi_{t_{2}-s_{2},t_{2}}(Y/\tau^{k})\to\pi_{t-s,t}(X/\tau^{k}\otimes Y/\tau^{k})\to\pi_{t-s,t-k+1}(X/\tau^{k}\otimes Y/\tau^{k})
factors through the epimorphism to
E_{k+1}^{s_{1},t_{1}}(X)\otimes_{\mathsf{Ab}}E_{k+1}^{s_{2},t_{2}}(Y)
since its kernel contains both
\ker(\cdot\tau^{k-1})\otimes\mathrm{id}
and
\mathrm{id}\otimes\ker(\cdot\tau^{k-1})
. On the other hand, the epi-mono factorization for the vertical right arrow is precisely
\pi_{t-s,t}(T/\tau^{k})\to E_{k+1}^{s,t}(T)\to\pi_{t-s,t-k+1}(T/\tau^{k})
. Thus, we obtain an induced map
F\colon E_{k+1}^{s_{1},t_{1}}(X)\otimes_{\mathsf{Ab}}E_{k+1}^{s_{2},t_{2}}(Y)\to E_{k+1}^{s,t}(T)
in
\mathsf{Ab}
, and the Leibniz rule for total differentials implies immediately the Leibniz rule for ordinary differentials.
∎

Remark 2.51
.

The same proof as in Theorem
2.50
also shows that, for every map
X_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes X_{n}\to T
, the induced multilinear pairings on
\pi_{**}(-/\tau^{r})
and on
E_{r+1}^{*,*}
satisfy the Leibniz rule for (total) differentials.

Remark 2.52
.

The family of pairings
\{F=F/\tau^{k}\}_{k\geqslant 1}
also acquires compatibility with
\tau^{m}
and
\rho
.

•

For
1\leqslant m\leqslant k\leqslant\infty,\alpha\in\pi_{**}(X/\tau^{k}),\beta\in\pi_{**}(Y/\tau^{k})
, we have
\rho^{k}_{m}F(\alpha,\beta)=F(\rho^{k}_{m}(\alpha),\rho^{k}_{m}(\beta))
. Actually, the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring map
\rho^{k}_{m}\colon\mathbb{1}/\tau^{k}\to\mathbb{1}/\tau^{m}
induces a symmetric monoidal transformation between lax symmetric monoidal endofunctors
-\otimes\mathbb{1}/\tau^{k}\to-\otimes\mathbb{1}/\tau^{m}
, and the relation follows by postcomposing this with
\pi_{**}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Gr}(\mathsf{Ab})^{\mathrm{Kos}})^{\mathrm{Day}}
.

•

For
0\leqslant m<k\leqslant\infty,\alpha\in\pi_{**}(X/\tau^{k-m}),\beta\in\pi_{**}(Y/\tau^{k})
, we have
F(\tau^{m}\alpha,\beta)=\tau^{m}F(\alpha,\rho^{k}_{k-m}(\beta))
. To prove this, it suffices to construct a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{k-m}\otimes Y/\tau^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{k-m}\otimes Y/\tau^{k-m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T/\tau^{k-m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X/\tau^{k}\otimes Y/\tau^{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T/\tau^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\otimes\rho^{k}_{k-m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{m}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
. As the upper-right composite of the tentative square can be identified with
X/\tau^{k-m}\otimes Y/\tau^{k}\to X/\tau^{k-m}\otimes_{\mathbb{1}/\tau^{k}}Y/\tau^{k}\cong(X\otimes Y)/\tau^{k-m}\xrightarrow{F}T/\tau^{k-m}\xrightarrow{\tau^{m}}T/\tau^{k}
, and the right-lower composite can be identified with
X/\tau^{k-m}\otimes Y/\tau^{k}\to X/\tau^{k-m}\otimes_{\mathbb{1}/\tau^{k}}Y/\tau^{k}\cong(X\otimes Y)/\tau^{k-m}\xrightarrow{\tau^{m}}(X\otimes Y)/\tau^{k}\xrightarrow{F}T/\tau^{k}
, one can fill this square with the homotopy
\tau^{m}\circ F\simeq F\circ\tau^{m}
.

Remark 2.53
.

Using the Beilinson
t
-structure, one can construct a
décalage
7
7

7

which stands for “page-turning” in SS
functor
\textrm{D\'{e}c}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
which sends
X
to the filtration
\textrm{D\'{e}c}(X)
with
\textrm{D\'{e}c}(X)(w)=(\tau^{B}_{\geqslant w}X)(-\infty)
. By construction,
\textrm{D\'{e}c}(X)(-\infty)=X(-\infty)
and
\textrm{D\'{e}c}(X)
is complete if
X
is complete. Moreover,
Déc
admits a lax symmetric monoidal structure according to
[
Ant24
, Lemma 8.7 and Theorem 8.9]
. One can then produce a different description of the standard SS:
E_{r}^{s,t}(X)=E_{2}^{s+(r-2)(t-s),s+(r-1)(t-s)}(\textrm{D\'{e}c}^{(r-2)}(X))
and
8
8

8

here
\textrm{D\'{e}c}^{(k)}
stands for
k
-fold iteration of
Déc
, in particular
\textrm{D\'{e}c}^{(0)}=\mathrm{id}

d_{r}
is given by reindexing
\delta_{1}=d_{2}
for
\textrm{D\'{e}c}^{(r-2)}(X)
, cf. Remark

and
[

, Theorem 4.11]
. Consequently, the lax monoidality of
