.

Remark 4.29
.

We end this section with some further historical comments and attributions concerning the generalized Mahowald trick. The relationship with Lin–Wang–Xu’s Adams SS version
[
Lin-Wang-Xu-kervaire
, Theorem 6.12]
has already been discussed above.

•

The idea behind the Mahowald trick goes back to Mahowald
[
MT67
]
.

In the terminology of the present paper, the rough intuition behind this primitive form of the Mahowald trick can be described as follows. Given a map
f\colon X\to Y
between filtered spectra and
Z=\cofib(f)
, there are two long exact sequences:

–

applying
\pi_{**}(-/\tau)
gives a long exact sequence on the
E_{2}
-pages of the standard spectral sequences of
X
,
Y
, and
Z
;

–

applying
\pi_{*}(-[\tau^{-1}])
gives a long exact sequence on the abutments of these standard spectral sequences.

In practice, there can be mismatches between the maps induced by
f
on the
E_{2}
-page and on the abutment. Concretely, suppose that
x\in E_{2}(X)
detects
\alpha
, and
y\in E_{2}(Y)
detects
\beta
, with a relation
f(\alpha)=\beta
in the abutment which is not visible on the
E_{2}
-page, for instance with
x\in\ker(f)
and
y\in\coker(f)
on
E_{2}
. Since the kernel and cokernel of
f
on the
E_{2}
-page, respectively on the abutment, are controlled by the
E_{2}
-page, respectively the abutment, of
Z
, one is led to expect that such mismatches should be accounted for by the information governing the passage from
E_{2}(Z)
to its abutment, namely the differentials in
E_{*}(Z)
.

This idea serves as a heuristic tool in many of Mahowald’s computations. For example, in
[
MT67
, Theorem 4.2.1]
, Mahowald and Tangora deduce
d_{4}(e_{0}g)=d_{0}Pd_{0}
in the Adams SS of
\mathbb{S}^{0}
through an argument guided by this philosophy, using what amounts to the triangle

\mathbb{S}^{1,2}_{\mathrm{H}\mathbb{F}_{2}}\xrightarrow{[h_{1}]}\mathbb{S}^{0,0}_{\mathrm{H}\mathbb{F}_{2}}\xrightarrow{i}\cofib(\eta)_{\mathrm{H}\mathbb{F}_{2}}\xrightarrow{p}\mathbb{S}^{2,2}_{\mathrm{H}\mathbb{F}_{2}}

to relate this differential to the
p
-extension on the abutment
e_{0}g[0]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.82}{$\displaystyle\rightsquigarrow$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.82}{$\textstyle\rightsquigarrow$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.82}{$\scriptstyle\rightsquigarrow$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.82}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightsquigarrow$}}}}}h_{0}^{2}m
.

•

Hill–Hopkins–Ravenel prove a version of the generalized Mahowald trick, more precisely a version of the Blueprint Theorem
4.20
, in
[
HHR-BPC4<1>
, Theorem 4.5]
. They use it to produce the stem-level extension input for their exotic transfer differentials in the
C_{4}
-slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle 1\rangle
.

The technical core of this result is
[
HHR-BPC4<1>
, Lemma 4.4]
, which plays a role analogous to our coherent Mahowald trick, Theorem
4.17
. As pointed out by Meier–Shi–Zeng
[
MSZ-localized-slice-SS
, Footnote 3]
, this lemma requires some additional care as stated. Rephrased in the language of the present paper, the issue is that the input in
[
HHR-BPC4<1>
, Lemma 4.4]
is not a
2
d cuDT in our sense, but only a
\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}\times\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}
-diagram in which every three consecutive arrows, either vertically or horizontally, form a DT or an ADT. Since such a diagram does not keep track of the nullhomotopy data, it need not be uniquely determined by its restriction to
\{0<1\}^{2}
.

For instance, let
X
be a
2
d cuDT with
X\langle 1,1\rangle=X\langle 3,2\rangle=X\langle 2,3\rangle=0
. After restricting
X
to
\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}\times\mathchoice{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.75346pt}{$\scriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.54247pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}{\mathord{\accentset{\smash{\raisebox{-0.45206pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightharpoonup$}}}{\mathbb{Z}}}}
, one may choose an automorphism
f
of
X\langle 2,2\rangle
and replace the map
X\langle 2,1\rangle\to X\langle 2,2\rangle
by its postcomposition with
f
. The resulting diagram
X^{\prime}
still satisfies the assumptions of
[
HHR-BPC4<1>
, Lemma 4.4]
and has the same restriction to
\{0<1\}^{2}
. However, Theorem
4.17
applied to
X
shows that the claimed conclusion for
X^{\prime}
can only hold after inserting the automorphism
f
in the appropriate place. Thus the statement does not hold in this level of generality without additional coherence data.

•

The geometric boundary theorem forms a related line of development. In its earlier forms, including Johnson–Miller–Wilson–Zahler
[
JMWZ
, Theorem 1.7]
, Bruner
[
Bru77
]
, and Ravenel
[
GreenBook
, Theorem 2.3.4]
, it compares algebraic boundary maps on the
E_{2}
-pages with geometric maps on abutments. Behrens
[
Behrens-EHP
, Lemma A.4.1]
formulates a version involving filtration jumps, bringing it closer to the intuition described above. This filtration-jump version is refined and corrected by Ma
[
Ma2
]
. For a more extensive history, see
[
Ma2
, Introduction]
.

In its most general form in
[
Ma2
]
, the geometric boundary theorem describes possible behaviors of the image of a differential
d(x)=x_{\infty}
in the standard SS of
X
along a map
f\colon X\to Y
in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
, or more precisely the
f
-extension of the target
x_{\infty}
, in terms of the differentials in
X
,
Y
, and
\cofib(f)
, together with the maps between their
E_{2}
-pages. In this formulation, bigraded stem lifts are built into the statement, while no-crossing hypotheses are not imposed.

The technical key of
[
Ma2
]
is
[
Ma1
, Lemma 5.1]
, which is essentially equivalent to our coherent Mahowald trick, Theorem
4.17
. The proof there is quite different from ours in Appendix
B
: it works directly in the quasicategorical model and verifies the required compatibilities through explicit choices of horn fillers, whereas our proof is model-independent and reduces the coherence check to a single family of concrete examples (or even to a single representative of the family).

As no-crossing conditions are out of the story, the geometric boundary theorems in
[
Ma2
]
are not directly comparable with Theorem
4.23
or Theorem
4.26
. Rather, they can be viewed as arising from iterations of the Blueprint Theorem
4.20
, together with the following two facts: for any map
f\colon X\to Y
in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
,

(a).

The map
f
is compatible with total differentials. In other words,
f(\delta_{r}^{\infty}[x])=\delta_{r}^{\infty}f[x]
in
\pi_{**}(Y)
for every
[x]\in\pi_{**}(X/\tau^{r})
and every
r\in\mathbb{N}_{\geqslant 1}
.

(b).

The map
f
is compatible with detection phenomena. In other words,
f(\iota[x])=\iota f[x]
in
\pi_{**}(Y[\tau^{-1}])
for every
[x]\in\pi_{**}(X)
, where
\iota
denotes the
\tau
-inversion map.

More precisely,
[
Ma2
, Theorem 1]
is an instance of (a). Theorem 2 of
[
Ma2
]
reduces to (a) once the extension
p_{*}y\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.82}{$\displaystyle\rightsquigarrow$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.82}{$\textstyle\rightsquigarrow$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.82}{$\scriptstyle\rightsquigarrow$}}}}{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.82}{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightsquigarrow$}}}}}x
is obtained using Theorem
4.20
; the same argument shows that
[
Ma2
, Theorem 5]
reduces to (b), that
[
Ma2
, Theorem 3]
reduces to
[
Ma2
, Theorem 2]
, and that
[
Ma2
, Theorem 4]
reduces to
[
Ma2
, Theorem 5]
.

Conversely, starting from a situation of type (a) or type (b), one may recursively replace an extension by an extension-differential-extension zigzag using Theorem
4.20
, which suggests an infinite family of possible geometric boundary patterns.

5   Applications in the equivariant slice SS

In the previous sections, we have developed the generalized Leibniz rule, the generalized Mahowald trick, and the Leibniz rule for total differentials in the realm of
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
using BIPWX’s cofiber-of-
\tau
formalism. We summarize these computational tools as the Burklund–Lin–Wang–Xu (BLWX) methods. In this section we apply BLWX methods to compute the equivariant slice spectral sequences of Hill–Hopkins–Ravenel theories. In §
5.1
we set up our convention for general
G
-equivariant filtrations and (
\mathrm{RO}(G)
-graded, Mackey-functor-valued) spectral sequences. In §
5.2
we introduce the equivariant slice SS and the HHR theories
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
. In §
5.3
and §
5.4
, we record known information for the slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}
. In §
5.5
, we use GLR and GMT to deduce exotic transfer differentials for the
C_{4}
-slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
in Theorem
5.29
and the subsequent examples. Finally, in §
5.6
we end the paper with a satisfactory summary of generating differentials in the
C_{4}
-slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle 1\rangle
.

5.1   Equivariant filtrations and equivariant SS

Let
G
be a finite group. In Appendix
we discuss Picard-graded SS in general, which in particular leads to a lax symmetric monoidal functor
E_{*}^{*,\filledstar}
sending a filtered genuine
G
-spectrum
X\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
to its
\mathrm{RO}(G)
-graded Mackey-functor-valued standard SS
E_{*}^{*,\filledstar}(X)
, cf. Example
. In this section, for better compatibility with convergence results in Theorem
2.34
, we phrase this construction in a slightly different (but equivalent) manner, which does not leave the realm of abelian groups.

Construction 5.1

(parametrized SS)
.

Suppose
\mathcal{C}\in\mathsf{Mod}_{\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}}(\mathsf{Pr}_{\mathrm{st}})
and
\mathcal{T}\subset\mathcal{C}
is a small full subcategory. We write
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}^{\mathsf{Fil}}\colon\mathcal{C}\to\mathsf{Fun}(\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp})
for the functor
X\mapsto(A\in\mathcal{T}\mapsto\psi_{A}^{\mathsf{Fil}}(X)=\mathrm{map}^{\mathsf{Fil}}(A,X))
. Moreover, we denote the composite

\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\psi^{\mathsf{Fil}}}\mathsf{Fun}(\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp})\xrightarrow{E_{*}^{*,*}}\mathsf{Fun}(\mathcal{T}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{SS})

by
X\mapsto\{E_{r}^{s,t,A}(X)\}_{r\geqslant 2,s,t\in\mathbb{Z},A\in\mathcal{T}}
, where
E_{r}^{s,t,A}(X)=E_{r}^{s,t}(\mathrm{map}^{\mathsf{Fil}}(A,X))
. By construction,
\psi^{\mathsf{Fil}}
controls both this parametrized SS and hidden extensions along the maps induced by each
A\to B
in
\mathcal{T}
. Furthermore, if
\mathcal{C}\in\mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Pr}_{\mathrm{st}})_{\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}/}
and
\mathcal{T}\subset\mathcal{C}
is a symmetric monoidal full inclusion, then
\psi^{\mathsf{Fil}}
and
E_{*}^{*,*}\circ\psi^{\mathsf{Fil}}
are lax symmetric monoidal under Day convolutions.

Construction 5.2

(equivariant SS)
.

Let
G
be a finite group.

•

The
\infty
-category
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
of filtered genuine
G
-spectra (equipped with the Day convolution symmetric monoidal structure) receives two symmetric monoidal left adjoints

i\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}\to\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G},\qquad j\colon\mathsf{Sp}^{G}=\mathsf{Fun}(\{0\},\mathsf{Sp}^{G})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{LKE}}\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}.

We write
\mathbb{1}=j(\mathbb{S}^{0})=i(\mathbb{S}^{0,0})\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
for the tensor unit. For
V\in\mathrm{RO}(G),w\in\mathbb{Z}
, we write
\mathbb{S}^{V,w}=j(\mathbb{S}^{V})\otimes i(\mathbb{S}^{0,w})
, and for
Y\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G},P\in\mathsf{Fin}_{G}
we write
Y[P]=Y\otimes j(\Sigma_{+}^{\infty}P)
.

•

Write
U\colon\mathsf{Sp}^{G}\to\mathsf{Sp},\mathbb{S}^{V}\mapsto\mathbb{S}^{|V|}
for the underlying spectra functor. Take
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{G}=\{\mathbb{S}^{V}\}_{V\in\mathrm{RO}(G)}\subset\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
and
\mathcal{T}_{G}=\{\mathbb{S}^{V,w}[P]\}_{V\in\mathrm{RO}(G),w\in\mathbb{Z},P\in\mathsf{Fin}_{G}}\subset\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
. Applying Construction
5.1
to
\mathcal{T}_{G}
, we obtain a lax symmetric monoidal functor
\psi=\psi^{\mathsf{Fil}}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}\to\mathsf{Fun}(\mathcal{T}_{G}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{SS})
. Furthermore, by precomposing the symmetric monoidal functor

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{G}\times\mathsf{Span}(\mathsf{Fin}_{G})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id},U)\times\mathrm{id}}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{G}\times\mathsf{Pic}(\mathsf{Sp})\times\mathsf{Span}(\mathsf{Fin}_{G})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\times\pi_{0}\times\mathrm{id}}\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{G}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\delta}\times\mathsf{Span}(\mathsf{Fin}_{G})\xrightarrow{j\times i\times j\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}}\mathcal{T}_{G}\times\mathcal{T}_{G}\times\mathcal{T}_{G}\xrightarrow{\otimes}\mathcal{T}_{G}.

we obtain a lax symmetric monoidal functor
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}\to\mathsf{Fun}({\mathcal{T}^{\prime}_{G}}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathsf{Mack}_{G}(\mathsf{SS}))
. Thus, for each
Y\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
we extract an
\mathrm{RO}(G)
-graded Mackey-functor-valued SS
16
16

16

or rather, an
\mathrm{RO}(G)
-family of SS-valued Mackey functors
.

We introduce some notational conventions: take
Y\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G},V\in\mathrm{RO}(G),P\in\mathsf{Fin}_{G}
, then

–

We write
\psi_{V}=\psi^{\mathsf{Fil}}_{\mathbb{S}^{V,|V|}}
and
E_{r}^{s,t,V}(Y[P])=E_{r}^{s,t,\mathbb{S}^{V,|V|}[P]}(Y)=E_{r}^{s,t}(\psi_{V}(Y[P]))
.

–

We further write
E_{r}^{s,V}(Y[P])=E_{r}^{s,0,V}(Y[P])
. Conversely, under this convention, we have
E_{r}^{s,t,V}(Y[P])\cong E_{r}^{s,t+V}(Y[P])
. The differential now goes as
d_{r}\colon E_{r}^{s,V}\to E_{r}^{s+r,V+r-1}
.

Therefore, it makes sense to say
\{E_{r}^{s,V}(Y[P])\}\Rightarrow\pi_{V-s}((Y[P])[\tau^{-1}])
if
Y
is
\tau
-complete and the corresponding (weak, whole-plane) obstructions vanish.

Remark 5.3
.

For
x\in E_{r}^{s,V}
, following the convention in
[
HSWX-BPC4<2>
]
we will write
|x|=(V-s,s)
and refer to this pair as the
bidegree
of
x
. This corresponds to the folklore convention for coordinates in the pictures of equivariant SS, cf. the figures in
[
HSWX-BPC4<2>
]
.

5.2   Equivariant slice SS and Hill–Hopkins–Ravenel theories

From this point to the end of this section, we assume each
\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
is localized at the prime
2
.

Recollection 5.4
.

Recall the notion of
regular slice filtrations
from
[
Ull13
,
HHR-book
]
.

•

The
regular slice cells
in
\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
are genuine
G
-spectra of the form
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}\mathbb{S}^{k\rho_{H}}
, where
H
is a subgroup of
G
,
\rho_{H}
is the regular representation of
H
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
.

•

Write
\mathsf{Sp}^{G}_{\geqslant n}\subset\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
for the full subcategory of
regular slice
n
-connective
G
-spectra
. Concretely, this is generated under colimits by the slice cells
\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}\mathbb{S}^{k\rho_{H}}
with
k|H|\geqslant n
. By
[
Hill-Yarnall
, Theorem A]
,
X\in\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
is regular slice
n
-connective iff for each
H\subset G
, the geometric fixed point
\Phi^{H}(X)\in\mathsf{Sp}
is
\lceil n/|H|\rceil
-connective.

•

The inclusion
j_{n}\colon\mathsf{Sp}^{G}_{\geqslant n}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
preserves all colimits, so it admits a right adjoint
P_{n}
. For
X\in\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
, we refer to
P_{n}X
as the
regular slice
n
-connective cover
. Since
\{\mathsf{Sp}^{G}_{\geqslant n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
forms a nested sequence with
\bigcap_{n}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}_{\geqslant n}=\{0\},\bigcup_{n}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}_{\geqslant n}=\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
, for each
X\in\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
we obtain a filtration

P_{\bullet}X\colon\cdots\to P_{n+1}X\to P_{n}X\to P_{n-1}X\to\cdots

so that
\lim_{n\to\infty}P_{n}X=0,\colim_{n\to-\infty}P_{n}X=X
. This is the
regular slice filtration
of
X
. Note that if
H\subset G
, then
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}(P_{\bullet}X)\cong P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}X)
. In particular,
(P_{\bullet}X)^{e}=\tau_{\geqslant\bullet}(X^{e})
.

•

The assignment
X\mapsto P_{\bullet}X
yields a functor
P_{\bullet}\colon\mathsf{Sp}^{G}\to\mathsf{Fil}(\mathsf{Sp}^{G})
. Furthermore, according to
[
BHS2
, Construction C.6 and Example C.7]
,
P_{\bullet}
is lax symmetric monoidal.

•

We refer to
\{E_{r}^{s,V}(P_{\bullet}X)\}_{r\geqslant 2,s\in\mathbb{Z},V\in\mathrm{RO}(G)}
as the
regular
G
-slice SS
of
X
. Furthermore, for each
H\subset G
, we refer to
\{E_{r}^{s,V}(P_{\bullet}X[G/H])\}_{r\geqslant 2,s\in\mathbb{Z},V\in\mathrm{RO}(G)}
as the
regular
H
-slice SS
of
X
. Note that
E_{r}^{s,V}(P_{\bullet}X[G/H])\cong E_{r}^{s,\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}V}(P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}X))
. In particular, for
H=e
the SS degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page.

In the following, we will suppress all “regular”, referring e.g. to
P_{\bullet}X
just as the
slice filtration
of
X
.

Remark 5.5
.

In
[
Car25
, § 3]
, the author equips
\mathsf{Fil}(\mathsf{Sp}^{G})
with a
G
-symmetric monoidal structure using equivariant Day convolution, and proves that
P_{\bullet}
is
G
-symmetric monoidal.

Recollection 5.6
.

We introduce the
Hill–Hopkins–Ravenel theories
following
[
Hu-Kriz
,
HHR-paper
]
.

•

The complex
j
-homomorphism
j_{\mathbb{C}}\colon\mathrm{BU}\to\mathrm{BGL}_{1}(\mathbb{S})
is
C_{2}
-equivariant for complex conjugation on the left and trivial action on the right. Taking the Thom construction (and employing its functoriality as in
[
ABGHR
, Definition 2.20]
), we obtain a Borel complete
C_{2}
-spectrum
\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}
. Concretely,
\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}^{e}\cong\mathrm{MU},\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}^{C_{2}}\cong\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}^{hC_{2}}
and
\Phi^{C_{2}}(\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}})\cong\mathrm{MO}
, cf.
[
Hu-Kriz
, § 2]
.

•

According to
[
Hu-Kriz
, Proof of Theorem 2.33]
, there is a
C_{2}
-equivariant map
e_{\mathbb{R}}\colon\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}
whose underlying map (resp. map on
C_{2}
-geometric fixed points) is the Quillen idempotent on
\mathrm{MU}
(resp. on
\mathrm{MO}
), and we write
\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}
for its mapping telescope. By construction,
\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}
is Borel complete (thus
\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}^{e}\cong\mathrm{BP},\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}^{C_{2}}\cong\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}^{hC_{2}}
), and
\Phi^{C_{2}}(\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}})\cong\mathrm{H}\mathbb{F}_{2}
.
Note that
e_{\mathbb{R}}
is an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring map due to
[
QZ25
, Theorem 6.2.1]
, so
\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}
is an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-algebra over
\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

•

For any group homomorphism
\rho\colon K\to G
, there is a unique symmetric monoidal sifted colimit preserving functor
\rho_{\otimes}\colon\mathsf{Sp}^{K}\to\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
which sends
\Sigma_{+}^{\infty}X
(
X\in\mathsf{Fin}_{K}
) to
17
17

17

here
\mathrm{Map}^{K}(G,X)
is the set of left
K
-equivariant maps from
G
to
X
, with the left
G
-action
(g_{0}\cdot f)(g)=f(gg_{0})

\Sigma_{+}^{\infty}\mathrm{Map}^{K}(G,X)
(cf.
[
BH21
, § 9.2]
). If
\rho
is surjective with
G=K/A
then
\rho_{\otimes}
recovers the geometric fixed point
\Phi^{A}
. If
\rho
is injective, then
\rho_{\otimes}=\mathrm{N}_{K}^{G}
is the
Hill–Hopkins–Ravenel norm
functor in
[
HHR-paper
, § 2.2.3]
. By construction,
(\mathrm{N}_{K}^{G}(X))^{e}\cong(X^{e})^{\otimes|G/K|}
for each
X\in\mathsf{Sp}^{K}
, and
\mathrm{N}_{K}^{G}(\mathbb{S}^{V})\cong\mathbb{S}^{\mathrm{Ind}_{K}^{G}(V)}
for each (virtual)
K
-representation
V
.

•

For any finite
2
-group
G
equipped with a fixed inclusion
C_{2}\subset G
, we can construct two
Hill–Hopkins–Ravenel (HHR) theories

\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}=\mathrm{N}_{C_{2}}^{G}\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}=\mathrm{N}_{C_{2}}^{G}\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}
. By symmetric monoidality,
\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\in\mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp}^{G})
and
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
is an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-algebra over
\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
.

•

According to
[
HHR-paper
, § 5]
, for
G=C_{2^{n}}
there is a family of classes
{\bar{t}}_{i}\in\pi_{i\rho_{2}}^{C_{2}}\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
(where
\rho_{2}
is the regular representation of
C_{2}
) lifting classes
t_{i}\in\pi_{2i}^{C_{2}}\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
for each
i\geqslant 1
, so that

\pi_{*}^{e}\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\cong\mathbb{Z}[t_{1},\gamma t_{1},\ldots,\gamma^{2^{n-1}-1}t_{1},t_{2},\gamma t_{2},\ldots]

where the generator
\gamma
of
G=C_{2^{n}}
acts on
\pi_{*}^{e}\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
by the formulas
\gamma\cdot\gamma^{k}t_{i}=\gamma^{k+1}t_{i}
for
0\leqslant k<2^{n-1}-1
and
\gamma\cdot\gamma^{2^{n-1}-1}t_{i}=-t_{i}
. Under this notation, we also have

\pi_{*}^{e}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\cong\mathbb{Z}[t_{1},\gamma t_{1},\ldots,\gamma^{2^{n-1}-1}t_{1},t_{3},\gamma t_{3},\ldots]

where the indices of
t_{i}
take values
i=2^{k}-1
for
k>0
.

•

For each
S\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
, the collection
\big\{{\bar{t}}_{i}\colon\mathbb{S}^{i\rho_{2}}\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{C_{2}}\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\big\}_{i\in S}
yields a map
18
18

18

here if
S\subset\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant 1}
is infinite, we write
\bigotimes_{i\in S}R_{i}
for
\colim_{S^{\prime}\subset S\text{ finite}}\bigotimes_{i\in S^{\prime}}R_{i}

\mathbb{1}[\{{\bar{t}}_{i}\}_{i\in S}]=\bigotimes_{i\in S}\mathrm{Free}_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}(\mathbb{S}^{i\rho_{2}})\to\bigotimes_{i\in S}\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{C_{2}}\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\to\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{C_{2}}\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}

in
\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{2}})
, which further gives rise to a composite

A_{S}=\mathbb{1}[\{G\cdot{\bar{t}}_{i}\}_{i\in S}]=\mathrm{N}_{C_{2}}^{G}\mathbb{1}[\{{\bar{t}}_{i}\}_{i\in S}]\to\mathrm{N}_{C_{2}}^{G}\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{C_{2}}\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\to\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}

in
\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{Sp}^{G})
, where the first map comes from monoidality of
\mathrm{N}_{C_{2}}^{G}
and the second map comes from the normed
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra structures on
\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}
and
\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}=\mathrm{N}_{C_{2}}^{G}\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}
due to
[
HM17
, Theorem 2.4]
and the norm-forgetful adjunction in
[
BH21
, Theorem 8.5 and the discussion after Remark 8.6]
. Also, each
A_{S}
admits a canonical
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-algebra map to
\mathbb{1}
which kills all
{\bar{t}}_{i}
. Thus, for
1\leqslant m\leqslant\infty
, it makes sense to introduce the following constructions:

–

We write
S^{\prime}_{m}=\mathbb{Z}_{>m},A^{\prime}_{>m}=A_{S^{\prime}_{m}}
and set
\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle=\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\otimes_{A^{\prime}_{>m}}\mathbb{1}
.

–

We write
S_{m}=\{2^{k}-1\}_{k>m},A_{>m}=A_{S_{m}}
and set
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle=\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\otimes_{A_{>m}}\mathbb{1}
.

We refer to these as the
truncated HHR-theories
.

Remark 5.7
.

By construction, all HHR theories, truncated or not, are modules over
\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
. Also, all HHR theories constructed out of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
are modules over
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
. Thus, the slice filtrations of all these objects are modules over
P_{\bullet}\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
(resp.
P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
), so due to Theorem
2.50
, we have Leibniz rules for ordinary and total differentials along the maps
P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\otimes P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\to P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
,
P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\otimes P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle)\to P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle)
, etc.

As the maps
P_{\bullet}\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\to P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}
and
P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\to P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle)
are all surjective on the slice
E_{2}
-page, we can treat the Leibniz rule from the module structure as if there is an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring structure on each
P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle)
which leads to the Leibniz rule on their equivariant slice SS, cf.
[
HSWX-BPC4<2>
, Remark 2.2]
.

5.3   Slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}

Fact 5.8

(Homology of a point)
.

The real representation ring
\mathrm{RO}(C_{2})
is generated by the trivial representation
1
and the sign representation
\sigma=\sigma_{2}
. The regular representation
\rho_{2}
decomposes as
\rho_{2}=1+\sigma_{2}
. Furthermore, for the constant Mackey functor
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
, the
\mathrm{RO}(C_{2})
-graded homology of a point takes the form
\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{\filledstar}=\pi^{C_{2}}_{\filledstar}(\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}})=\mathbb{Z}[a_{\sigma},u_{2\sigma}]/(2a_{\sigma})\oplus\mathcal{N}_{2}
, where

•

a_{\sigma}
comes from the
Euler class
with
|a_{\sigma}|=-\sigma
.

•

u_{2\sigma}
comes from the
Thom class
with
|u_{2\sigma}|=2-2\sigma
.

•

\mathcal{N}_{2}
stands for the
negative cone
19
19

19

which we ignore in this paper
square-zero ideal.

For reference, see
[
Dug05
, Theorem 2.8 and Appendix B]
, where the earlier computation is traced back to Stong; for a modern account in the notation used here, see also
[
HHR-BPC4<1>
, §3]
. Throughout, we use the multiplicative shorthand
a_{k\sigma}=a_{\sigma}^{k}
and
u_{2k\sigma}=u_{2\sigma}^{k}
; the same convention applies to all Euler classes, Thom classes, and related notation.

Recollection 5.9

(degree filtration)
.

Take
G=C_{2}
and
1\leqslant m\leqslant\infty
.

•

We write
\mathbb{Z}_{[1,m]}=\{k\in\mathbb{Z}\mid 1\leqslant k<m+1\}
and take
\mathcal{I}_{m}=\mathrm{Map}^{\mathrm{fs}}(\mathbb{Z}_{[1,m]},\mathbb{N})
, the set of maps
\alpha\colon\mathbb{Z}_{[1,m]}\to\mathbb{N}
so that
\alpha(k)=0
for all but finitely many
k
. We equip
\mathcal{I}_{m}
with the alphabetic order and pointwise addition. For
\alpha\in\mathcal{I}_{m}
, we write
|\alpha|=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{[1,m]}}\alpha(k)(2^{k}-1)\in\mathbb{N}
.

•

We denote by
A_{C_{2},\leqslant m}
the
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring
\bigotimes_{1\leqslant k<m+1}\mathrm{Free}_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}(\mathbb{S}^{(2^{k}-1)\rho_{2}})
in
\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{2}}
. Due to
[
HA
, Proposition 4.1.1.18]
,
A_{C_{2},\leqslant m}\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathcal{I}_{m}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha|\rho_{2}}
. Therefore,
A_{C_{2},\leqslant m}
comes with an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-multiplicative
degree filtration

A_{C_{2},\leqslant m,\bullet}
in which
A_{C_{2},\leqslant m,w}=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathcal{I}_{m},|\alpha|\geqslant w/2}\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha|\rho_{2}}
.

Remark 5.10
.

Conceptually,
A_{C_{2},\leqslant m,\bullet}\cong\bigotimes_{1\leqslant k<m+1}\mathrm{Free}_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}(\mathbb{S}^{(2^{k}-1)\rho_{2},2^{k+1}-2})
in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{2}}
.

Theorem 5.11

(Slice theorem)
.

For each
1\leqslant m\leqslant\infty
,
\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\langle m\rangle\otimes_{A_{C_{2},\leqslant m}}A_{C_{2},\leqslant m,\bullet}\cong P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\langle m\rangle)
. Moreover, on the associated graded we have
\mathrm{gr}_{\bullet}(P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\langle m\rangle))\cong\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathrm{gr}_{\bullet}(A_{C_{2},\leqslant m,\bullet})
.

Proof.

This is essentially the result of
[
HHR-paper
, § 6]
for
G=C_{2}
, first proved in
[
Hu-Kriz
, § 3]
.
∎

Corollary 5.12
.

The
E_{2}
-page of the
C_{2}
-slice SS of
X=\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\langle m\rangle
takes the form

E_{2}^{s,V}(P_{\bullet}X)\cong\pi_{V-s}(\mathrm{gr}_{|V|}P_{\bullet}X)\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathcal{I}_{m}\\
|\alpha|=|V|/2\end{subarray}}\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{V-s-|\alpha|\rho_{2}}

in particular
E_{2}^{s,V}=0
if
|V|
is odd. Concretely, each slice cell
\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha|\rho_{2}}
contributes a copy of
\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{\filledstar-|\alpha|\rho_{2}}
with generator
{\bar{t}}^{\alpha}=\prod_{k\geqslant 1}({\bar{t}}_{2^{k}-1})^{\alpha(k)}
at bidegree
(|\alpha|\rho_{2},0)=(|\alpha|+|\alpha|\sigma,0)
, so the full
E_{2}
-page is a polynomial ring
\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{\filledstar}[{\bar{t}}_{1},{\bar{t}}_{3},{\bar{t}}_{7},\ldots,{\bar{t}}_{2^{m}-1}]
, with
|a_{\sigma}|=(-\sigma,1)
,
|u_{2\sigma}|=(2-2\sigma,0)
and
|{\bar{t}}_{2^{k}-1}|=(2^{k}-1+(2^{k}-1)\sigma,0)
.

Theorem 5.13

(Slice differential theorem)
.

In the
C_{2}
-slice SS of
X=\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\langle m\rangle
, we have a family of nontrivial differentials
d_{2^{k+1}-1}(u_{2^{k}\sigma})={\bar{t}}_{2^{k}-1}a_{(2^{k+1}-1)\sigma}
for
1\leqslant k\leqslant m
. Together with the fact that
1
,
a_{\sigma}
, and
{\bar{t}}_{2^{k}-1}
are permanent cycles for
1\leqslant k\leqslant m
, this family determines all differentials in this SS by the Leibniz rule.

Proof.

This follows from
[
HHR-paper
, Theorem 9.9]
for
G=C_{2}
, also first proved in
[
Hu-Kriz
, § 3]
.
∎

5.4   Slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}
: known partial data

From here to the end of this section, we will work with
G=C_{4}
unless otherwise specified. We write
\mathrm{Res}
for
\mathrm{Res}^{C_{4}}_{C_{2}}
, and similarly for
\mathrm{Ind},\mathrm{N},\mathrm{tr},\mathrm{res}
. We start with the slice
E_{2}
-pages.

Fact 5.14

(Homology of a point)
.

The real representation ring
\mathrm{RO}(C_{4})
is generated by the trivial representation
1
, the sign representation
\sigma=\sigma_{4}
and the
\pi/2
-rotation representation
\lambda
, and the regular representation
\rho_{4}
decomposes as
\rho_{4}=1+\sigma+\lambda
. Moreover, for the constant
C_{4}
-Mackey functor
\underline{\mathbb{Z}}
:

•

\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{\filledstar}=\pi^{C_{4}}_{\filledstar}(\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}})=\mathbb{Z}[a_{\lambda},a_{\sigma},u_{\lambda},u_{2\sigma}]/(4a_{\lambda},2a_{\sigma},u_{\lambda}a_{2\sigma}-2u_{2\sigma}a_{\lambda})\oplus\mathcal{N}_{4}
, where

–

a_{\lambda},a_{\sigma}
come from
Euler classes
with
|a_{\lambda}|=-\lambda,|a_{\sigma}|=-\sigma
.

–

u_{\lambda},u_{2\sigma}
come from
Thom classes
with
|u_{\lambda}|=2-\lambda,|u_{2\sigma}|=2-2\sigma
.

–

\mathcal{N}_{4}
stands for the
negative cone
20
20

20

which we ignore in this paper
square-zero ideal.

•

For each
V\in\mathrm{RO}(C_{4})
,
\pi^{C_{4}}_{V}(\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}[C_{4}/C_{2}])\cong\pi^{C_{2}}_{\mathrm{Res}(V)}(\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}})
as in Fact
5.8
. Therefore,

\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}[C_{4}/C_{2}]_{\filledstar}=\pi^{C_{4}}_{\filledstar}(\mathrm{Ind}\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}))\cong\mathbb{Z}[a_{2\sigma_{2}},u_{2\sigma_{2}},u_{\sigma}^{\pm 1}]/(2a_{2\sigma_{2}})\oplus\mathcal{N}^{\prime}_{2}.

Here
u_{\sigma}
comes from the isomorphism
\Sigma^{1-\sigma}X[C_{4}/C_{2}]\cong\mathrm{Ind}(\mathrm{Res}(\mathbb{S}^{1-\sigma})\otimes\mathrm{Res}(X))\cong\mathrm{Ind}\mathrm{Res}X\cong X[C_{4}/C_{2}]
for each
X\in\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{4}}
. We take the convention that
|a_{2\sigma_{2}}|=-\lambda,|u_{2\sigma_{2}}|=2-\lambda
and
|u_{\sigma}|=1-\sigma
. The natural
\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{\filledstar}\!
-action on
\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}[C_{4}/C_{2}]_{\filledstar}
is presented by the formulas
a_{\lambda}\cdot x=a_{2\sigma_{2}}x,u_{\lambda}\cdot x=u_{2\sigma_{2}}x,a_{\sigma}\cdot x=x
and
u_{2\sigma}\cdot x=u_{\sigma}^{2}x
.

For reference, see
[
HHR-BPC4<1>
, § 3]
.

Remark 5.15
.

For any
\mathrm{RO}(C_{2})
-graded abelian group
A=A_{\filledstar}
, we write
\mathrm{Ev}(A)
for its restriction to the subring of
\mathrm{RO}(C_{2})
generated by
1
and
2\sigma_{2}
, and we denote by
\mathrm{Ind}_{\filledstar}(A)=\mathrm{Ev}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}[u_{\sigma}^{\pm 1}]
the
\mathrm{RO}(C_{4})
-graded abelian group with
x\in A_{p+2q\sigma_{2}}
in
\mathrm{RO}(C_{4})
-degree
p+q\lambda
and
|u_{\sigma}|=1-\sigma
. Under these notations,
\pi^{C_{4}}_{\filledstar}(\mathrm{Ind}(X))\cong\mathrm{Ind}_{\filledstar}(\pi^{C_{2}}_{\filledstar}(X))
, in particular
\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}[C_{4}/C_{2}]_{\filledstar}=\mathrm{Ind}_{\filledstar}(\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}})_{\filledstar})
.

Lemma 5.16
.

Suppose
H\subset G
is an index-
2
subgroup. Then for
X,Y\in\mathsf{Sp}^{H}
, there is a natural identification
\mathrm{N}_{H}^{G}(X\oplus Y)\cong\mathrm{N}_{H}^{G}(X)\oplus\mathrm{N}_{H}^{G}(Y)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(X\otimes Y)
.

Proof.

This follows from the model category discussion in
[
HHR-paper
, Proposition A.37]
. Alternatively, according to
[
EH23
, Proposition 3.4.19]
, from the distributivity diagram in
\mathsf{Fin}_{G}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G/H\sqcup G/H\sqcup(G/H\sqcup G/H)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G/G\sqcup G/G\sqcup G/H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G/H\sqcup G/H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G/H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G/G}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1},p_{2},p_{1},p_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{H}^{G}\sqcup i_{H}^{G}\sqcup\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id},i_{H}^{G})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{H}^{G}}

we can extract a commutative pentagon

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Sp}^{H}\times\mathsf{Sp}^{H}\times(\mathsf{Sp}^{H}\times\mathsf{Sp}^{H})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Sp}^{G}\times\mathsf{Sp}^{G}\times\mathsf{Sp}^{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Sp}^{H}\times\mathsf{Sp}^{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Sp}^{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Sp}^{G}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{N}_{H}^{G}\times\mathrm{N}_{H}^{G}\times\otimes}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\oplus\mathrm{id}\oplus\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p_{1},p_{2},p_{1},p_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{N}_{H}^{G}}

Thus the identification
\mathrm{N}_{H}^{G}(X\oplus Y)\cong\mathrm{N}_{H}^{G}(X)\oplus\mathrm{N}_{H}^{G}(Y)\oplus\mathrm{Ind}_{H}^{G}(X\otimes Y)
follows.
∎

Recollection 5.17

(degree filtration)
.

We write
A_{C_{4},\leqslant m}=\mathrm{N}_{C_{2}}^{C_{4}}(A_{C_{2},\leqslant m})
. Using Lemma
5.16
and the fact that
\mathrm{N}
commutes with filtered colimits, we deduce that (under the alphabetic order on
\mathcal{I}_{m}
):

A_{C_{4},\leqslant m}\cong\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathcal{I}_{m}}\mathrm{N}(\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha|\rho_{2}})\oplus\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{I}_{m}\\
\alpha>\beta\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha|\rho_{2}}\otimes\mathbb{S}^{|\beta|\rho_{2}})\cong\bigoplus_{\alpha\in\mathcal{I}_{m}}\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha|\rho_{4}}\oplus\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{I}_{m}\\
\alpha>\beta\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha+\beta|\rho_{2}})

here
\rho_{4}
is the regular representation for
C_{4}
. Therefore,
A_{C_{4},\leqslant m}
admits a (
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-multiplicative)
degree filtration

A_{C_{4},\leqslant m,\bullet}
with
A_{C_{4},\leqslant m,w}=\bigoplus_{|\alpha|\geqslant w/4}\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha|\rho_{4}}\oplus\bigoplus_{\alpha>\beta,|\alpha+\beta|\geqslant w/2}\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha+\beta|\rho_{2}})
.

Theorem 5.18

(slice theorem)
.

Take
1\leqslant m\leqslant\infty
. Treating
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
as an
A_{C_{4},\leqslant m}
-module using the
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring map
A_{C_{4},\leqslant m}\to\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}
in the construction of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
, we have

\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle\otimes_{A_{C_{4},\leqslant m}}A_{C_{4},\leqslant m,\bullet}\cong P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle),\qquad\mathrm{gr}_{\bullet}(P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle))\cong\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathrm{gr}_{\bullet}(A_{C_{4},\leqslant m,\bullet}).

Proof.

This is essentially the result in
[
HHR-paper
, § 6]
for
G=C_{4}
.
∎

Remark 5.19
.

Conceptually, under the terminology of filtered HHR normed functor
\mathrm{N}_{C_{2}}^{G}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{2}}\to\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}
coming from the
G
-symmetric monoidal structure in Remark
5.5
,
A_{C_{4},\leqslant m,\bullet}\cong\mathrm{N}_{C_{2}}^{C_{4}}(A_{C_{2},\leqslant m,\bullet})
. In general, for
G=C_{2^{k}}
, write
A_{G,\leqslant m,\bullet}=\mathrm{N}_{C_{2}}^{G}(A_{C_{2},\leqslant m,\bullet})
, then we also have slice theorems

\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle\otimes_{A_{G,\leqslant m}}A_{G,\leqslant m,\bullet}\cong P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle),\qquad\mathrm{gr}_{\bullet}(P_{\bullet}(\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle))\cong\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}\otimes\mathrm{gr}_{\bullet}(A_{G,\leqslant m,\bullet})

following the same discussion as in
[
HHR-paper
, § 6]
. The concrete enumeration for slice cells (in particular the induced cells) appears to be involved, cf.
[
HHR-paper
, § 2.4.1]
.

Corollary 5.20
.

•

Note that
\mathrm{Res}(A_{C_{4},\leqslant m,\bullet})\cong A_{C_{2},\leqslant m,\bullet}^{\otimes 2}
due to the general formula
\mathrm{Res}^{G}_{H}\circ\mathrm{N}^{G}_{H}\cong(-)^{\otimes|G/H|}
. Therefore, the
E_{2}
-page of the
C_{2}
-slice SS of
X=\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
takes the form

E_{2}^{s,V}(P_{\bullet}X[C_{4}/C_{2}])\cong\pi_{\mathrm{Res}(V)-s}(\mathrm{gr}_{|V|}\mathrm{Res}(P_{\bullet}X))\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{I}_{m}\\
|\alpha+\beta|=|V|/2\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}})_{\mathrm{Res}(V)-s-|\alpha+\beta|\rho_{2}}.

In particular,
E_{2}^{s,V}(P_{\bullet}X[C_{4}/C_{2}])=0
if
|V|
is odd. Concretely,
E_{2}^{*,\filledstar}(\mathrm{Res}(P_{\bullet}X))
is the
\mathrm{RO}(C_{2})
-graded polynomial ring
\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}})_{\filledstar}[{\bar{t}}_{1},\gamma{\bar{t}}_{1},{\bar{t}}_{3},\gamma{\bar{t}}_{3},\ldots,{\bar{t}}_{2^{m}-1},\gamma{\bar{t}}_{2^{m}-1}]
, where
|a_{\sigma_{2}}|=(-\sigma_{2},1)
,
|u_{2\sigma_{2}}|=(2-2\sigma_{2},0)
, and
|{\bar{t}}_{2^{k}-1}|=|\gamma{\bar{t}}_{2^{k}-1}|=((2^{k}-1)+(2^{k}-1)\sigma_{2},0)
, while
E_{2}^{s,\filledstar}(P_{\bullet}X[C_{4}/C_{2}])=\mathrm{Ind}_{\filledstar}(E_{2}^{s,\filledstar}(\mathrm{Res}(P_{\bullet}X)))
in terms of Remark
5.15
.

•

The
E_{2}
-page of the
C_{4}
-slice SS of
X=\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
takes the form

E_{2}^{s,V}(P_{\bullet}X)\cong\pi_{V-s}(\mathrm{gr}_{|V|}P_{\bullet}X)\cong\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha\in\mathcal{I}_{m}\\
|\alpha|=|V|/4\end{subarray}}\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{V-s-|\alpha|\rho_{4}}\oplus\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{I}_{m},\alpha>\beta\\
|\alpha+\beta|=|V|/2\end{subarray}}\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}})_{\mathrm{Res}(V)-s-|\alpha+\beta|\rho_{2}}.

In particular,
E_{2}^{s,V}(P_{\bullet}X)=0
if
|V|
is odd. Concretely, there are contributions from
norm (i.e. noninduced) cells

\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha|\rho_{4}}
and
induced cells

\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha+\beta|\rho_{2}})
:

–

For each
\alpha\in\mathcal{I}_{m}
, the norm cell
\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha|\rho_{4}}
gives rise to a copy of
\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{\filledstar-|\alpha|\rho_{4}}
generated by the
norm class
21
21

21

Norm classes come from the normed algebra structure map
\mathrm{N}(\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}))\to\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}

\mathrm{N}({\bar{t}}^{\alpha})=\prod_{k\geqslant 1}\mathrm{N}({\bar{t}}_{2^{k}-1})^{\alpha(k)}
in bidegree
(|\alpha|\rho_{4},0)=(|\alpha|+|\alpha|\sigma+|\alpha|\lambda,0)
. In other words, all classes from noninduced cells in the
E_{2}
-page form a polynomial subring
\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}}_{\filledstar}[\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3},\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{7},\ldots,\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1}]
, where
|a_{\sigma}|=(-\sigma,1),|a_{\lambda}|=(-\lambda,2),|u_{2\sigma}|=(2-2\sigma,0),|u_{\lambda}|=(2-\lambda,0)
and
\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{k}-1}=\mathrm{N}({\bar{t}}_{2^{k}-1})
is of bidegree
(2^{k}-1+(2^{k}-1)\sigma+(2^{k}-1)\lambda,0)
.

–

For each pair
\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal{I}_{m}
such that
\alpha>\beta
, the induced cell
\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbb{S}^{|\alpha+\beta|\rho_{2}})
gives rise to a copy of
\mathrm{Ind}_{\filledstar}(\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{H}\underline{\mathbb{Z}})_{\filledstar-|\alpha+\beta|\rho_{2}})
spanned by
transfer classes
22
22

22

Transfer classes come from the transfer map
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}[C_{4}/C_{2}]\to\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}

\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}^{\alpha}\gamma\bar{t}^{\beta}u_{2p\sigma_{2}}a_{q\sigma_{2}})u_{k\sigma}

of bidegree
(|\alpha|+|\beta|+2p+k-k\sigma+\frac{|\alpha|+|\beta|-2p-q}{2}\lambda,q)
, where
\gamma\bar{t}^{\beta}=\prod_{k\geqslant 1}(\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{k}-1})^{\beta(k)}
. Furthermore, the classes from the induced cell form an ideal of the
E_{2}
-page. Concretely, multiplication involving transfer classes is computed by the Frobenius relation and the fact
\mathrm{res}(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{i})=\bar{t}_{i}\gamma\bar{t}_{i}
, for instance
\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{1}a_{\sigma_{2}})\cdot\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3}u_{3\lambda}a_{\lambda}=\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{1}\bar{t}_{3}\gamma\bar{t}_{3}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{\sigma}^{-3}u_{3\lambda}a_{\lambda}=\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{1}\bar{t}_{3}\gamma\bar{t}_{3}u_{6\sigma_{2}}a_{3\sigma_{2}})u_{-3\sigma}
. In particular, transfer classes are
a_{\sigma}
-torsion since
\mathrm{res}(a_{\sigma})=0
.

Remark 5.21
.

All classes from the induced cells are transfer classes, but the converse might not hold true, for instance
2=\mathrm{tr}(1)
comes from the norm cell
\mathbb{S}^{0}
.

We then record two sources of
C_{4}
-slice differentials, the
transfer differentials
and the
sheared differentials
. Transfer differentials come from a complete description of the
C_{2}
-slice SS.

Fact 5.22
.

The unit of the norm-forgetful adjunction
\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)})
commutes with the real Quillen idempotent
e_{\mathbb{R}}
in the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-algebras in
\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{2}}
. Therefore, it induces an
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring map between the summands
\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)})
, which further leads to a map of
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-multiplicative filtrations
P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathrm{Res}(P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)})
.

Proof.

The claim above actually holds true in a stronger sense, with
\mathbb{E}_{1}
replaced by the
C_{2}
-operad
\mathbb{E}_{\rho}
in the sense of
[
Hill-Erho
]
, where
\rho=\rho_{2}
is the regular representation of
C_{2}
. In fact, the second claim follows directly from the first one through a slice connectivity argument. To prove the first claim, we systematically use the discussions in
[
QZ25
]
. As
\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)})
is a strongly even
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{C_{2}}
-ring spectrum in the sense of
[
QZ25
, Definition 5.1.3]
, an application of
[
QZ25
, Theorem 6.2.1]
shows it suffices to construct a homotopy that fills the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MU}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MU}\otimes\mathrm{MU}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MU}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{MU}\otimes\mathrm{MU}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e\,\otimes\,e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

in
\mathsf{Alg}_{\mathbb{E}_{2}}(\mathsf{Sp})
, where
e
is the (nonequivariant) Quillen idempotent and
u
is the underlying map of
\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)})
. On the other hand, after passing to Borel completion, the norm-forgetful adjunction for
C_{2}\subset C_{4}
can be identified with the LKE-forgetful adjunction between
\mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})^{\mathrm{B}C_{2}}
and
\mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Sp})^{\mathrm{B}C_{4}}
. Therefore, the underlying map
u
of the adjunction unit
\mathrm{MU}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathrm{Res}(\mathrm{MU}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)})
is the unit-insertion map
\mathrm{MU}\to\mathrm{MU}^{\otimes|C_{4}/C_{2}|}\cong\mathrm{MU}\otimes\mathrm{MU}
associated to the finite set inclusion
*=C_{2}/C_{2}\subset C_{4}/C_{2}
singled out by the subgroup inclusion
C_{2}\subset C_{4}
, and hence is functorial in
\mathrm{MU}
as an
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-ring. Thus, the above square has a canonical filler as long as
e
is a map of
\mathbb{E}_{2}
-ring spectra, which is true by
[
ChadwickMandell-EnGenera
, Corollary 1.3]
.
∎

Theorem 5.23
.

In the
C_{2}
-slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
, there are differentials for each
1\leqslant k\leqslant 2m
that take the form

d_{2^{k+1}-1}(u_{2^{k}\sigma_{2}})={\bar{v}}_{k}a_{(2^{k+1}-1)\sigma_{2}}

here
{\bar{v}}_{k}
is the image of the
k
-th
Araki generator
23
23

23

in our convention this is precisely
{\bar{t}}_{2^{k}-1}\in\pi_{(2^{k}-1)\rho_{2}}^{C_{2}}\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\langle 2m\rangle
in
\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}
under the map in Fact
5.22
. Concretely, write
I_{k}
for the ideal
(2,{\bar{v}}_{1},\ldots,{\bar{v}}_{k-1})\subset\pi_{\filledstar}^{C_{2}}(\mathrm{Res}\,\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle)
, then

{\bar{v}}_{k}=\begin{cases}\bar{t}_{2^{k}-1}+\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{k}-1}+\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{j}-1}\cdot\bar{t}_{2^{k-j}-1}^{2^{j}}\mod I_{k},&1\leqslant k\leqslant m.\\
\sum_{j=k-m}^{m}\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{j}-1}\cdot\bar{t}_{2^{k-j}-1}^{2^{j}}\mod I_{k},&m+1\leqslant k\leqslant 2m.\end{cases}

Furthermore, the pattern for all differentials in this SS is determined by the Leibniz rule, the fact that
a_{\sigma_{2}}
and all
\bar{t}_{2^{k}-1},\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{k}-1}
are permanent cycles, and the above family of differentials.

Proof.

This is
[
DLS-vanishing-lines
, Theorem 2.3]
in the case
G=C_{4}
. The formulas for
\bar{v}_{k}
come from
[
BHSZ
, Theorem 1.1]
for
G=C_{4}
or the discussion in
[
HSWX-BPC4<2>
, § 2.4]
.
∎

Remark 5.24
.

Under the map
\mathrm{tr}\colon P_{\bullet}X[C_{4}/C_{2}]\to P_{\bullet}X
, every differential
d_{r}(x)=y
in the
C_{2}
-slice SS gives rise to a family of
transfer differentials

d_{r}(\mathrm{tr}(x)u_{k\sigma})=\mathrm{tr}(y)u_{k\sigma}
in the
C_{4}
-slice SS. By Theorem
5.23
, each essential transfer differential for
X=\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
has length
2^{k+1}-1
for some
1\leqslant k\leqslant 2m
.

Sheared differentials, on the other hand, come from the transchromatic “shearing” isomorphism between certain regions of two different slice spectral sequences. To describe this we shall introduce the following notion:

Definition 5.25
.

Suppose
\mathcal{L}:s=k(t-s)+b
is a line on the
(t-s,s)
-plane, where
k>-1
.

•

We say
F\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
is
\mathcal{L}
-connective
if
E_{2}^{s,t}(F)=0
whenever
(t-s,s)
is above
\mathcal{L}
, i.e.
s>k(t-s)+b
.

•

We say
f\colon X\to Y
in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
is
\mathcal{L}
-connective
if
F=\fib(f)
is
\mathcal{L}
-connective. In other words,
f
is
\mathcal{L}
-connective if
E_{2}^{s,t}(X)\to E_{2}^{s,t}(Y)
is an isomorphism for
(t-s,s)
above
\mathcal{L}
and a surjection for
(t-s,s)
on
\mathcal{L}
. In this case, by induction (cf.
[
MSZ-stratification
, Proof of Theorem 2.6]
), every essential differential in
\{E_{r}^{s,t}(X)\}
whose source is on or above the line
\mathcal{L}
goes to an essential differential in
\{E_{r}^{s,t}(Y)\}
, and conversely each essential differential in
\{E_{r}^{s,t}(Y)\}
whose source is on or above the line
\mathcal{L}
comes from an essential differential in
\{E_{r}^{s,t}(X)\}
. We refer to this situation as an
isomorphism of SS on or above the line
\mathcal{L}
.

Remark 5.26
.

In general, for each line
\mathcal{L}
on the
(t-s,s)
-plane whose slope
\neq-1
, we say
F\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
is
\mathcal{L}
-connective
if
E_{2}^{s,t}(F)=\pi_{t-s,t}(F/\tau)=0
whenever
(t-s,s)
and
(k,-k)
lie in different connected components of
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\mathcal{L}
for
k\gg 0
.

•

The collection of
\mathcal{L}
-connective objects forms the connective part of a
t
-structure on
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
, whose
(-1)
-coconnective part consists of complete filtrations
F
so that
E_{2}^{s,t}(F)=0
whenever
(t-s,s)
lies on
\mathcal{L}
or in the same connected component of
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\mathcal{L}
as
(k,-k)
for
k\gg 0
. In fact, there is a
t
-structure on
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}_{\hat{\tau}}
in which
F
is (co)connective iff each spectrum
(F/\tau)(w)
is
\lceil x(w)\rceil
-(co)connective, where
x(w)
is the horizontal coordinate of the intersection point in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
between the line
\mathcal{L}
and the unique line of slope
-1
passing through
(w,0)
. This is straightforward under the identification between complete filtered spectra and coherent cochain complexes
[
Ariotta
, Theorem 3.19]
, and the desired
t
-structure on
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
follows by gluing it with the trivial
t
-structure on
\mathsf{Sp}
(in which all spectra are connective) along the recollement in Remark
2.12
using
[
Ariotta
, Theorem 5.10]
.

•

For
\mathcal{L}\colon s=-2(t-s)
, this recovers the Beilinson
t
-structure in Construction
2.43
.

•

By construction, for every
F\in\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}
,
E_{2}^{s,t}(\tau_{\geqslant 0}^{\mathcal{L}}F)=0
if
(t-s,s)
and
(k,-k)
lie in different connected components of
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\mathcal{L}
for
k\gg 0
, otherwise it is isomorphic to
E_{2}^{s,t}(F)
. Thus, this is not the same as the linear
t
-structure in
[
Lee-Levy
, Example 2.8]
or
[
CD26
, Definition 3.1]
.

•

For
\mathcal{L}
with slope
<-1
, we can reproduce the discussion in Definition
5.25
, but here an
\mathcal{L}
-connective map
f\colon X\to Y
would induce an
isomorphism of SS on or below the line
\mathcal{L}
.

Theorem 5.27

(transchromatic shearing isomorphism & slice recovery theorem)
.

Take
1\leqslant m<\infty
. Recall the functor
\psi_{V}=\psi^{\mathsf{Fil}}_{V}\colon\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{G}\to\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp},Y\mapsto\mathrm{map}^{\mathsf{Fil}}(\mathbb{S}^{V,|V|},Y)
in Construction
5.2
.

•

There is an isomorphism
P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle[a_{\sigma}^{-1}]\cong\mathrm{Infl}(\mathrm{Sl}_{2}(P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\langle m\rangle[a_{\sigma_{2}}^{-1}]))
in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{4}}
, for which
\mathrm{Infl}=\mathrm{Infl}_{C_{4}/C_{2}}^{C_{4}}
is the inflation and
\mathrm{Sl}_{2}
is the
2
-fold slowdown in Construction
2.43
.

•

For any
X\in\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{2}}
,
V\in\mathrm{RO}(C_{2})
, the map
\psi_{V}(P_{\bullet}X)\to\psi_{V}(P_{\bullet}X[a_{\sigma_{2}}^{-1}])
is
\mathcal{L}^{C_{2}}_{V,0}
-connective and the map
\psi_{V}(P_{\bullet}X[a_{\sigma_{2}}^{-1}])\to 0
is
\mathcal{L}^{C_{2}}_{V,1}
-connective, where

\mathcal{L}^{C_{2}}_{V,0}\colon s=0,\qquad\mathcal{L}^{C_{2}}_{V,1}\colon s=(t-s)+2|V^{C_{2}}|-|V|.

•

For any
X\in\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{4}},V\in\mathrm{RO}(C_{4})
, the maps
\psi_{V}(P_{\bullet}X)\to\psi_{V}(P_{\bullet}X[a_{\lambda}^{-1}])
,
\psi_{V}(P_{\bullet}X[a_{\lambda}^{-1}])\to\psi_{V}(P_{\bullet}X[a_{\sigma}^{-1}])
and
24
24

24

the map
X[a_{\lambda}^{-1}]\to X[a_{\sigma}^{-1}]
exists since
\mathbb{1}[a_{\lambda}^{-1}]=\widetilde{\mathrm{E}\mathcal{F}\!}_{\mathrm{triv}}
with
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{triv}}=\{e\}
and
\mathbb{1}[a_{\sigma}^{-1}]=\widetilde{\mathrm{E}\mathcal{F}\!}_{\mathrm{pr}}
with
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{pr}}=\{e,C_{2}\}

\psi_{V}(P_{\bullet}X[a_{\sigma}^{-1}])\to 0
are respectively
\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{V,0},\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{V,1},\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{V,2}
-connective, where

\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{V,0}\colon s=0,\quad\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{V,1}\colon s=(t-s)+2|V^{C_{2}}|-|V|,\quad\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{V,2}\colon s=3(t-s)+4|V^{C_{4}}|-|V|.

Consequently, for each
V\in\mathrm{RO}(C_{4}),V^{\prime}\in\mathrm{RO}(C_{2})
and
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}
with
t-s\geqslant 0
,
\{E_{r}^{s,t+V}(P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle)\}
vanishes above the line
\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{V,2}
and
\{E_{r}^{s,t+V^{\prime}}(P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\langle m\rangle)\}
vanishes above the line
\mathcal{L}^{C_{2}}_{V^{\prime},1}
. Furthermore, from these comparisons we deduce a
“shearing” isomorphism
between
\{E_{r}^{s,t+V^{C_{2}}}(P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}_{\mathbb{R}}\langle m\rangle)\}
on or above the line
s=0
and
\{E_{r^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime},t^{\prime}+V}(P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle)\}
on or above the line
\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{V,1}
, under which

\displaystyle E_{r}^{s,t+V^{C_{2}}}\leftrightarrow E_{2r-1}^{s+t+2|V^{C_{2}}|-|V|,2t+V+2|V^{C_{2}}|-|V|},\ \ d_{r}\leftrightarrow d_{2r-1},

\displaystyle a_{\sigma_{2}}\leftrightarrow a_{\sigma},\ \ u_{2\sigma_{2}}\leftrightarrow u_{2\sigma},\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \bar{t}_{2^{k}-1}\leftrightarrow\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{k}-1}a_{(2^{k}-1)\lambda}.

Thus, there is a
sheared differential

d_{2^{k+2}-3}(u_{2^{k}\sigma})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{k}-1}a_{(2^{k}-1)\lambda}a_{(2^{k+1}-1)\sigma}
for each
1\leqslant k\leqslant m
in the
C_{4}
-slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
, and these generate all differentials on or above
\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{\filledstar,1}
by the Leibniz rule.

Proof.

The isomorphism in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{4}}
is
[
MSZ-transchromatic
, Theorem 4.1]
for
G=C_{4}
, and the
\mathcal{L}
-connectivity results follow from
[
MSZ-stratification
, Theorem 2.1]
for
G=C_{2},C_{4}
. The precise translation along the shearing isomorphism is due to
[
MSZ-transchromatic
, Theorem 5.5 and Theorem 6.2]
. Finally, the sheared differentials were first established in
[
HHR-paper
, Theorem 9.9]
, and they can be deduced from Theorem
5.13
via the shearing isomorphism (as is recorded in
[
MSZ-transchromatic
, Theorem 6.7]
). The latter approach also proves these generate all differentials on or above the lines
\mathcal{L}_{\filledstar,1}^{C_{4}}
by the Leibniz rule.
∎

5.5   Exotic transfer differentials

In this subsection we use Burklund–Lin–Wang–Xu methods to construct new families of differentials in the
C_{4}
-slice spectral sequence of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
. Building on the generalized Leibniz rule and the generalized Mahowald trick from the previous sections, we “splice” transfer differentials with sheared differentials to produce
exotic transfer differentials
. This recovers, and then extends to all heights
m
, the exotic transfer differentials found for
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle 1\rangle
in
[
HHR-BPC4<1>
]
.

Definition 5.28
.

Take
1\leqslant m<\infty
.

•

We write
B_{m}=\mathbb{Z}[T_{1},T_{3},\ldots,T_{2^{m}-1}]
for the graded polynomial ring with
|T_{2^{i}-1}|=2^{i}-1
.

•

We write
R_{m}=\mathbb{Z}[\bar{t}_{1},\gamma\bar{t}_{1},\bar{t}_{3},\gamma\bar{t}_{3},\ldots,\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1},\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}]
for the graded ring with
|\bar{t}_{2^{i}-1}|=|\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{i}-1}|=2^{i+1}-2
. We equip it with the
C_{2}=\{e,\gamma\}
action by
\gamma(\bar{t}_{2^{i}-1})=\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{i}-1},\gamma(\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{i}-1})=-\bar{t}_{2^{i}-1}
.

Theorem 5.29

(exotic transfer paradigm)
.

Take
1\leqslant m<\infty
. Suppose for
h\in\{1,\ldots,2m\}
, there is a homogeneous polynomial
P\in B_{m}
with
|P|=p\geqslant 2^{h-1}
and two homogeneous classes
x,c\in R_{m}
with
|x|=4p-2^{h+1}+2,|c|=4p
, so that in
R_{m}
we have

x\cdot\bar{v}_{h}=P(\bar{t}_{1}\gamma\bar{t}_{1},\bar{t}_{3}\gamma\bar{t}_{3},\ldots,\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1})+c+\gamma(c)\mod I_{h}=(2,\bar{v}_{1},\ldots,\bar{v}_{h-1}).

Then for each
q\in\{1,\ldots,m\},j\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a differential in the
C_{4}
-slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle

d_{2^{h+1}+2^{q+2}-5}(\mathrm{tr}(xa_{\sigma_{2}})u_{2^{h-1}\lambda}u_{k\sigma})=P(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1},\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3},\ldots,\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1})\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{q}-1}u_{2^{q+1}\!j\sigma}a_{(2^{h}+2^{q}-1)\lambda}a_{(2^{q+1}-2)\sigma}

here
k=k(q,p,j)=2^{q+1}\!j+2^{q}-p-1\in\mathbb{Z}
(which can be negative).

In fact, Theorem
5.29
produces families of
transchromatic differentials
in the
C_{4}
-slice spectral sequence of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
, namely differentials whose source and target lie on opposite sides of one of the lines
\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{\filledstar,1}
. Figure
7
is a schematic illustration of the proof below: Roughly speaking, the generalized Mahowald trick turns a certain
sheared differential
d({z}_{1})={z}
into a

\mathrm{tr}
-extension from
{y}_{1}
to
{y}
, and the generalized Leibniz rule blends this with a certain
transfer differential
d({w}_{1})={y}_{1}
to produce the desired exotic transfer differential
d({w})={y}
.

Proof.

As
C_{4}/C_{2}
is the unit sphere of
\sigma\in\mathrm{RO}(C_{4})
, the cofiber of
\mathrm{tr}\colon\mathbb{S}^{0}[C_{4}/C_{2}]\to\mathbb{S}^{0}
in
\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{4}}
is the Euler class
a_{\sigma}\colon\mathbb{S}^{0}\to\mathbb{S}^{\sigma}
. Taking the Spanier–Whitehead dual, the cofiber of
a_{\sigma}
is the composite
\mathbb{S}^{\sigma}\to\mathbb{S}^{\sigma}[C_{4}/C_{2}]\cong\mathbb{S}^{1}[C_{4}/C_{2}]
, where the first map is
\mathrm{res}
and the second isomorphism is
u_{\sigma}^{-1}
. Thus,

\mathbb{S}^{0}[C_{4}/C_{2}]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{S}^{0}\xrightarrow{a_{\sigma}}\mathbb{S}^{\sigma}\xrightarrow{u_{\sigma}^{-1}\mathrm{res}}\mathbb{S}^{1}[C_{4}/C_{2}]

forms a distinguished triangle in
\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{4}}
. It further induces a distinguished triangle in
\mathsf{Fil}\mathsf{Sp}^{C_{4}}

Y[C_{4}/C_{2}]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}Y\xrightarrow{a_{\sigma}}\Sigma^{\sigma,0}Y\xrightarrow{u_{\sigma}^{-1}\mathrm{res}}\Sigma^{1,0}Y[C_{4}/C_{2}]

(
\blacklozenge
)

for
Y=P_{\bullet}\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
, from which the expected differential will be deduced via GLR and GMT.

The source of our expected differential sits in bidegree
(V+2^{h+1}+1,1)
for
V=(2p+k-2^{h+1})-k\sigma+(p-2^{h})\lambda
. A preimage of this class along the map
\mathrm{tr}
(more precisely, along the map
\mathrm{tr}(-)u_{k\sigma}
) is
w_{1}=xu_{2^{h}\sigma_{2}}a_{\sigma_{2}}
, which supports a differential in the
C_{2}
-slice SS

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}d_{2^{h+1}-1}(xu_{2^{h}\sigma_{2}}a_{\sigma_{2}})=x\bar{v}_{h}a_{2^{h+1}\sigma_{2}}=(P(\bar{t}_{1}\gamma\bar{t}_{1},\ldots,\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1})+c+\gamma(c))a_{2^{h+1}\sigma_{2}}}

according to Theorem
5.23
. Here we get a strict equality on the
E_{2^{h+1}-1}
-page since
2a_{\sigma_{2}}=0
and each
\bar{v}_{i}a_{2^{h+1}\sigma_{2}}
(
i<h
) is already wiped out in some earlier page. The target of this

d_{2^{h+1}-1}
is equal to

\mathrm{res}(P(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1})u_{(k+p+1)\sigma}a_{2^{h}\lambda}+\mathrm{tr}(c)u_{(k+1)\sigma}a_{2^{h}\lambda}).

The classes inside
\mathrm{res}(-)
sit in bidegree
((2p+k+1)-(k+1)\sigma+(p-2^{h})\lambda,2^{h+1})
, which is on the line
\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{V-\sigma+1,1}\colon s=(t-s)
. By Theorem
5.27
, the differential on these classes can be read off in the localized slice SS
E_{*}^{*,\filledstar}(Y[a_{\sigma}^{-1}])
. The transfer class is thus a permanent cycle as every transfer class is
a_{\sigma}
-torsion. On the other hand, as
k+p+1=2^{q}(2j+1)
, the norm class supports a differential

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d_{2^{q+2}-3}(P(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1})u_{(k+p+1)\sigma}a_{2^{h}\lambda})

\displaystyle=P(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1})u_{2^{q+1}\!j\sigma}a_{2^{h}\lambda}\cdot d_{2^{q+2}-3}(u_{2^{q}\sigma})

\displaystyle=P(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1})\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{q}-1}u_{2^{q+1}\!j\sigma}a_{(2^{h}+2^{q}-1)\lambda}a_{(2^{q+1}-1)\sigma}

which is again due to Theorem
5.27
. Applying the generalized Mahowald trick (i.e. Theorem
4.23
) to the
\psi_{V}
-image of (

⧫

‣
5.5
), we can convert this
d_{2^{q+2}-3}
differential
into an extension

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}d_{2^{q+2}-4}^{\mathrm{tr}(-)u_{k\sigma},E_{2^{q+2}-2}}(\text{target of the above }d_{2^{h+1}-1})=P(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1})\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{q}-1}u_{2^{q+1}\!j\sigma}a_{(2^{h}+2^{q}-1)\lambda}a_{(2^{q+1}-2)\sigma}}

Here the no-crossing conditions are satisfied as there is no filtration jump along
\mathrm{res}
and
a_{\sigma}
. Furthermore, the target of the above
d_{2^{h+1}-1}
sits in bidegree
(2p+(2p-2^{h+1}\sigma_{2}),2^{h+1})
, which is on the line
\mathcal{L}^{C_{2}}_{V_{1},1}:s=(t-s)
for
V_{1}=\mathrm{Res}(V)=(2p-2^{h+1})+(2p-2^{h+1})\sigma_{2}
. On the
E_{2}
-page there is already no class above this bidegree by Theorem
5.27
, so we can apply Theorem
3.12
to upgrade the
E_{2^{q+2}-2}
-page extension to an extension on the
E_{\infty}
-page. The expected differential now follows from an application of the generalized Leibniz rule (i.e. Theorem
3.24
) along the
\psi_{V}
-image of the map
\mathrm{tr}
(or more precisely,
\mathrm{tr}(-)u_{k\sigma}
) to the

d_{2^{h+1}-1}
differential
and the

E_{\infty}
-page extension with filtration jump
2^{q+2}-4
, in which no-crossing conditions are satisfied since there is no filtration jump on the source and the
E_{\infty}
-page extension on the target has no-crossing for degree reasons.
∎

.

In the following, we list several families arising from Theorem
5.29
as illustrative examples.

Example 5.30
.

Take
1\leqslant m<\infty
. We first construct a collection of examples that work for each pair
(h,q)
with
2\leqslant h\leqslant 2m,1\leqslant q\leqslant m
. Note that for each
z,w\in R_{m}
, we have
(z+w)\cdot\gamma(z+w)=z\cdot\gamma(z)+w\cdot\gamma(w)+(1+\gamma)(z\cdot\gamma(w))
mod
2
. Thus for
x=\gamma(\bar{v}_{h})
, there exists
c_{h}\in R_{m}
so that
\gamma(\bar{v}_{h})\cdot\bar{v}_{h}=P_{h}(\bar{t}_{1}\gamma\bar{t}_{1},\ldots,\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1})+c_{h}+\gamma(c_{h})
mod
I_{h}
, where

P_{h}(T_{1},\ldots,T_{2^{m}-1})=\begin{cases}\sum_{j=1}^{h-1}T_{2^{j}-1}T_{2^{h-j}-1}^{2^{j}},&2\leqslant h\leqslant m.\\
\sum_{j=h-m}^{m}T_{2^{j}-1}T_{2^{h-j}-1}^{2^{j}},&m+1\leqslant h\leqslant 2m.\end{cases}

In particular,
p=|P_{h}|=2^{h}-1
. Consequently, for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

d_{2^{h+1}+2^{q+2}-5}(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{v}_{h}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{2^{h-1}\lambda}u_{(2^{q+1}\!j+2^{q}-2^{h})\sigma})=P_{h}(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1})\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{q}-1}u_{2^{q+1}\!j\sigma}a_{(2^{h}+2^{q}-1)\lambda}a_{(2^{q+1}-2)\sigma}.

Example 5.31
.

There is a refined choice for splicing the longest transfer differential and the longest sheared differential for
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
: note that in
R_{m}

\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}-1}\cdot\bar{v}_{2m}=\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}-1}\cdot\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}}\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}=\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}}\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}}\mod I_{2m}.

Thus for
h=2m
, the tuple
x=\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}-1},P=T_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}},c=0
satisfies the hypotheses of Theorem
5.29
, where
p=|P|=2^{2m}-2^{m}
. Taking
q=m
, we deduce that

d_{2^{2m+1}+2^{m+2}-5}(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}-1}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{2^{2m-1}\lambda}u_{(2^{m+1}\!j+2^{m+1}-2^{2m}-1)\sigma})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}+1}u_{2^{m+1}\!j\sigma}a_{(2^{2m}+2^{m}-1)\lambda}a_{(2^{m+1}-2)\sigma}

for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Setting
j=2^{m}-1
and multiplying both sides by
\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}+1}a_{(2^{2m}-2^{m}-1)\lambda}
yields a differential in the integer-graded page of the
C_{4}
-slice SS

\displaystyle d_{2^{2m+1}+2^{m+2}-5}(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}+1}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}-1}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{2^{2m-1}\lambda}u_{(2^{2m}-1)\sigma}

\displaystyle a_{(2^{2m}-2^{m}-1)\lambda})

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m+1}+2}u_{(2^{2m+1}-2^{m+1})\sigma}a_{(2^{2m+1}-2)\lambda}a_{(2^{m+1}-2)\sigma}

whose source lies in bidegree
(2^{2m+2}-2^{m+1}-1,2^{2m+1}-2^{m+1}-1)
.

•

When
m=1
, the family of differentials above specializes to

d_{11}(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{1}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{2\lambda}u_{(4j-1)\sigma})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}^{3}u_{4j\sigma}a_{5\lambda}a_{2\sigma},\ \ j\in\mathbb{N}

which generates all essential

differentials in the (positive cone of the)

-slice SS of

under the Leibniz rule, and the integer-graded differential becomes

d_{11}(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}^{3}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{1}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{2\lambda}u_{3\sigma}a_{\lambda})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}^{6}u_{4\sigma}a_{6\lambda}a_{2\sigma}

out of bidegree
(11,3)
, which is the first essential
d_{11}
in the (positive cone of the) integer-graded
C_{4}
-slice SS, cf.
[

, Theorem 3.18 and Figure 9]
.

•

When
m=2
, the family of differentials above specializes to

d_{43}(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{3}^{3}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{8\lambda}u_{(8j-9)\sigma})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3}^{5}u_{8j\sigma}a_{19\lambda}a_{6\sigma},\ \ j\in\mathbb{N}

which generates all essential
d_{43}
differentials in the (positive cone of the)
C_{4}
-slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle 2\rangle
under the Leibniz rule, and the integer-graded differential becomes

d_{43}(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3}^{5}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{3}^{3}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{8\lambda}u_{15\sigma}a_{11\lambda})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3}^{10}u_{24\sigma}a_{30\lambda}a_{6\sigma}

out of bidegree
(55,23)
, which is the first essential
d_{43}
in the (positive cone of the) integer-graded
C_{4}
-slice SS, cf.
[
HSWX-BPC4<2>
, Theorem 9.5 and Figure 35]
.

In both known cases, this is the family of transchromatic differentials whose targets lie highest above the “transchromatic line”
\mathcal{L}^{C_{4}}_{\filledstar,1}
. Indeed, this assertion will be true for all
m\geqslant 1
for degree reasons as long as we know such differentials are essential.

Example 5.32
.

Not all families of differentials produced by Theorem
5.29
are guaranteed to be essential. Take
2\leqslant m<\infty
and consider the same identity as in Example
5.31
above

\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}-1}\cdot\bar{v}_{2m}=\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}}\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}}\mod I_{2m}.

Setting
h=2m
,
x=\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}-1}
,
P=T_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}}
,
c=0
, and
q=m-1
, we also deduce a family of differentials

d_{2^{2m+1}+2^{m+1}-5}(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}-1}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{2^{2m-1}\lambda}u_{(2^{m}\!j+2^{m+1}-2^{2m}-2^{m-1}-1)\sigma})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m-1}-1}\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m}}u_{2^{m}\!j\sigma}a_{(2^{2m}+2^{m-1}-1)\lambda}a_{(2^{m}-2)\sigma}

for
j\in\mathbb{N}
. These are usually not essential differentials, for instance:

•

When
m=2
, the family specializes to

d_{35}(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{3}^{3}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{8\lambda}u_{(4j-11)\sigma})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3}^{4}u_{4j\sigma}a_{17\lambda}a_{2\sigma}

for
j\in\mathbb{N}
. None of these is essential because of the family

d_{27}(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{1}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{6\lambda}u_{(4j-5)\sigma})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3}^{2}u_{4j\sigma}a_{13\lambda}a_{2\sigma}

in the
C_{4}
-slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle 2\rangle
, cf.
[
HSWX-BPC4<2>
, Proposition 10.5]
.

Example 5.33
.

For some
m\geqslant 2
, there exists a homogeneous class
c_{0}\in R_{m}
such that

\bar{t}_{1}\bar{v}_{2m-1}=\bar{t}_{2^{m-1}-1}^{2^{m-1}-1}\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m-1}-1}^{2^{m-1}-1}\cdot\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}\gamma\bar{t}_{2^{m}-1}+c_{0}+\gamma(c_{0})\mod I_{2m-1}.

This is the case for
m=2
due to
[
HSWX-BPC4<2>
, Theorem 2.11]
, and also for
m=3,4
by a brute force Gröbner basis computation (cf. the scripts in
[
WuZenodoC2Poly2026
]
). We expect such
c_{0}
to exist for all
m\geqslant 2
.

In the case such
c_{0}
exists, the collection
x=\bar{t}_{1}
,
P=T_{2^{m-1}-1}^{2^{m-1}-1}T_{2^{m}-1}
,
c=c_{0}
satisfies the hypotheses of Theorem
5.29
, here
p=|P|=2^{2m-2}
. Taking
q=m-1
, we deduce that

d_{2^{2m}+2^{m+1}-5}(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{1}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{2^{2m-2}\lambda}u_{(2^{m}\!j+2^{m-1}-2^{2m-2}-1)\sigma})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m-1}-1}^{2^{m-1}}\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1}u_{2^{m}\!j\sigma}a_{(2^{2m-1}+2^{m-1}-1)\lambda}a_{(2^{m}-2)\sigma}

for each
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Setting
j=2^{m}-2^{m-2}
and multiplying both sides by
\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m-1}+1}a_{(2^{2m-2}+2^{m-1}-1)\lambda}
yields a differential in the integer-graded page of the
C_{4}
-slice SS

\displaystyle d_{2^{2m}+2^{m+1}-5}(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m-1}+1}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{1}a_{\sigma_{2}})

\displaystyle u_{2^{2m-2}\lambda}u_{(2^{2m-1}+2^{m-1}-1)\sigma}a_{(2^{2m-2}+2^{m-1}-1)\lambda})

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m-1}-1}^{2^{m-1}}\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{2^{m}-1}^{2^{m-1}+2}u_{(2^{2m}-2^{2m-2})\sigma}a_{(2^{2m}-2^{2m-2}+2^{m}-2)\lambda}a_{(2^{m}-2)\sigma}

whose source lies in bidegree
(2^{2m+1}-2^{2m-1}+2^{m}-1,2^{2m-1}+2^{m}-1)
.

•

When
m=2
, the family of differentials above specializes to

d_{19}(\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{1}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{4\lambda}u_{(4j-1)\sigma})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}^{2}\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3}u_{4j\sigma}a_{9\lambda}a_{2\sigma},\ \ j\in\mathbb{N}

which generates all essential transchromatic
d_{19}
differentials in the (positive cone of the)
C_{4}
-slice SS of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle 2\rangle
under the Leibniz rule, and the integer-graded differential becomes

d_{19}(\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3}^{3}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{t}_{1}a_{\sigma_{2}})u_{4\lambda}u_{9\sigma}a_{5\lambda})=\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}^{2}\bar{\mathfrak{d}}_{3}^{4}u_{12\sigma}a_{14\lambda}a_{2\sigma}

out of bidegree
(27,11)
, which is the first essential transchromatic
d_{19}
in the (positive cone of the) integer-graded
C_{4}
-slice SS, cf.
[
HSWX-BPC4<2>
, Theorem 10.5 and Figure 31]
.

5.6   Epilogue: classifying slice differentials for
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle 1\rangle

In general, the slice SS of each
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(G)\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle m\rangle
collapses after finitely many pages. Also, there are various periodicities in these slice SS, cf.
[
DHLLSWX-periodicity
]
. Together, these imply that in each slice SS there are finitely many
generating differentials
that give rise to all differentials under the Leibniz rule.

Figure 8:
The
C_{4}
-slice spectral sequence of
\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle 1\rangle
, starting from its
E_{5}
-page.

(whose first column is from
[

) gives a full list of generating differentials in the
C_{4}

Y=\mathrm{BP}^{(\mkern-4.22mu(C_{4})\mkern-3.5mu)}\langle 1\rangle
with short indications for the proofs of their existence:
